# 🚀 Release Notes

What shipped, newest first — the full story of a fix lives in its [bug report](../bugs/README.md).

## When to update this file

- **Not on every commit.** Skip `chore` / `docs` / `refactor` / internal-only changes.
- Finished a **user-facing feature**? Add one line under **`## Unreleased` → Features**.
- **Agent Dashboard shows what the agents really do** — one card per AI agent (Atlas, Sherlock, Nightwatch, Companion, Screen review, Public Ask, Browser, MCP) with health, current work, calls, errors and tokens; a log drawer per agent with history, live tail, links to reports/alerts/traces and full chat transcripts; an admin Usage & decisions tab. The fake Benchmarks, Leaderboard, rating, YAML import/export and the unused agent runtime are gone. In plain words: you can now see each agent's work and logs, not placeholders · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261005a_agent_turns.sql`
- Finished a **user-facing fix**? Log it in the [bug tracker](../bugs/README.md) first, then add
  one line under **`## Unreleased` → Bug fixes** with its `[BUG-XXXX]` link. Features and fixes
  stay in separate lists — never mix them.
- **Cutting a build?** Rename `## Unreleased` → `## build <NNNN> — <YYYY-MM-DD>`, start a fresh
  empty `## Unreleased` above it, and set the fixed-in build on those bugs in the tracker.
- **Every build section ends with `### Upgrade`**: what an operator has to do to run this
  build — database migrations to
  apply, new `.env` keys, which services restart (and which the deploy script does *not*
  restart), proxy / Grafana files to hand-deploy, and the exact deploy commands with their
  parameters. That block is generated, not remembered:
  `python3 scripts/release/upgrade_notes.py [<previous-tag>]` reads the git diff since the last
  build and prints both headings; paste them, then write the plain-words paragraph. Keep the
  Unreleased section's copy current when a change adds a migration or a new key, so the block
  is never written from memory at cut time.
- **Feature needs a DB migration?** The entry **must** flag it by ending with
  ` · 🗄 DB migration \`<file>\`` — the dated file in `setup/db/migrations/` (or the feature's own
  `db/` dir, e.g. `features/cicd/db/`). `update_core.sh` deploys code but does **not** apply
  migrations, so shipping the code without running the migration first breaks the feature; the flag
  is the reminder to apply it on the DB before/with the deploy. When several entries share one
  migration, flag only the entry that introduces it.
  **The marker is per-database, not per-release.** A migration run on our own deployment is *not*
  done — it must be applied to every database the release reaches, including each customer install
  and any second environment. Treat a flagged entry as outstanding until every one of them has had
  it run.

Format each line as `- **Short title** — short clause · [BUG-XXXX](../bugs/BUG-XXXX-<date>-<slug>.md) · \`shortHash\` · 🗄 DB migration \`<file>\``
— a bug fix links to **its own bug file**, not the index. Drop the `[BUG-XXXX]` link for features
(which have no bug entry), and the `🗄 DB migration` marker for anything that doesn't touch the
schema. Entries that exist only on this branch (`feat/demo`) sit at the end of each list.

---

## Unreleased

### Features
- **AI cost is filled automatically, in our database and in Langfuse** — OpenRouter calls carry the cost OpenRouter bills (also through the OpenAI passthrough); other OpenRouter models are priced daily from OpenRouter's public model list, Anthropic/OpenAI models from Langfuse's list, the MiniMax coding plan counts as "included in plan" (cost 0), local PCs are free, and a price you type always wins. Langfuse generations now receive the same cost, and models Langfuse does not know are added to its list. Settings → AI → Prices shows the source of each price; the Providers section shows "included in plan". In plain words: the cost tiles stop saying "—" and the numbers in our page and in Langfuse agree.
- **The server's public name comes from `.env`, not from the code** — scripts, the localize tools, the fleet-health units and the Grafana installer read `SERVER_PUBLIC_URL` (scripts: `VPT_SERVER_URL`, `VPT_MCP_URL`, `FLEET_HEALTH_SERVERS`) instead of a hardcoded `rpitest` address, and the CORS default no longer lists it. New `.env` keys: `SERVER_PUBLIC_URL` on every server, `FLEET_HEALTH_SERVERS` on the server that runs the fleet-health timers; the two `vpt-fleet-health*.service` units are hand-installed and must be re-copied. In plain words: renaming a server no longer means searching the repo. [TASK-92](../tasks/TASK-92-server-url-single-source.md)
- **AI usage page answers "where does the AI go and is it worth it"** — Overview with four tiles (requests, useful %, saved by memory, cost) and one bar per category (Ask AI, Discard script, Discard incident, Script creation, Screen recognition, Transcripts & translation, Showcase / games, Web lookup) split useful / wasted / saved, plus who uses it (Atlas, Sherlock, Forge, vpt-monitor, a script…); an errors badge and an Errors tab instead of the banner; one filter bar (period, team, project, user, provider, model, result) kept in the URL for every tab; Usage with a model-chain line per row; every configured provider listed; Training as cards; an older server shows "—" or "does not have AI usage yet" instead of crashing; Settings → AI gets a Team and a Project dropdown; cloud model prices are fetched from Langfuse's price list when it has one, local endpoints cost nothing. In plain words: the page tells you where AI requests, tokens and money go and how much of it was wasted, without having to read every table
- **AI usage page rebuilt to be readable** — tabs (Overview, Features, Callers, Calls, Cost, Training) in the URL; the health banner shows one line per cause ("OpenRouter has no credit left — affects …", since / last, Top up credit); four tiles with ▲/▼ against the previous period; a provider list with answered / failed bars; one-line tables with human labels, features grouped by family; a detail with a headline sentence, a chart and the model chain (tried / answered / failed per model, "no backup configured"); every section folds with no animation; failures read "Couldn't load … Retry". In plain words: you can see at a glance which provider is broken, what it affects and what to do · 🗄 DB migration `20261012e_ai_calls_rollup_times.sql` (optional: exact first / last times)
- **Localize shows its evidence and takes a correction** — the Localize popover gets a More step in place (no more dialog over the stream): the captured frame, the closest screen, Copy for the live and the saved fingerprint, and "Wrong screen?" with "Unknown screen" as the first answer. In plain words: when Localize gets it wrong you can see why and copy a report of the right answer
- **A drone says why it has no picture instead of "Stream restart required"** — a Tello is its own Wi-Fi access point and only streams while it flies, so the Device preview used to call a switched-off drone a stuck FFmpeg and, worse, refused the click: with no stream the preview showed an error toast, leaving the re-host dialog and the flight panel unreachable exactly when you need them. The preview now polls the drone's own status and scan, says "Can't find Tello Wi-Fi — please power on the drone" when the AP is not in range, joins the AP by itself the moment it appears (only while the device preview is open, so browsing Device Control never changes a host's network), and always lets the click through to the Re-host stream dialog. The stuck-stream latch no longer applies to drones at all — the tile keeps waiting for frames instead of giving up. In plain words: a drone that is off, landed, or flying now looks different in each state, and you can always open its stream dialog · [BUG-0490](../bugs/BUG-0490-2026-10-12-tello-pair-routes-400-and-missing-controller.md)
- **Group control keeps the streams already playing** — on Device Control, Group → Control now moves each device's live tile player into the group window (and back on release), the same way a single tile already hands its stream to the stream modal; focusing a cell no longer restarts its stream either. Devices that were snapshots or off the current page still start their own stream. In plain words: opening group control shows the video straight away instead of reloading every stream
- **AI health alerts and monthly budgets** — the usage page flags a feature whose error rate passes 5 % or whose backup answers more than 10 % of requests in the last hour, and shows each team's month spend, forecast and budget; a budget can block the team's AI calls once used up · `d51648f88` · 🗄 DB migration `20261012d_ai_budgets.sql`
- **AI cost, priced when the call is made** — each request keeps its cost (provider-reported, else the dated price table in Settings → AI → Prices), so a price change never rewrites history; run reports show "AI used by this run", and the usage page shows what caches and rules saved · `626b842a8` · 🗄 DB migration `20261012c_ai_prices.sql`
- **AI usage shows who called each model** — every AI request is logged with its caller (web user, script run, monitoring, agent chat, MCP, public Ask), model, effort, tokens, speed, errors and cost; group by team, user, script, host, device or origin, filter, export CSV, see provider health and region, and open the run report or the conversation behind a number · `b14343514` · 🗄 DB migration `20261012b_ai_calls.sql`
- **Monitoring → AI usage** — one read-only page that shows, per AI feature, which provider and model answers, with which effort, how often the backup took over, and the requests, tokens, speed and errors; group by feature, model, provider or team over 1 h to 30 days · `eb717adec`
- **Bluetooth remote panel opens instantly on a connected STB** — the panel remembers the last BLE status of each device for the browser session, so opening it again (or re-taking control) shows the remote at once instead of "Loading BLE status…"; one background check confirms the link and a real drop still shows within the usual 4 s. In plain words: no loading flash before the remote on an STB that is already connected
- **Group control of phones: click the stream to tap every phone** — a click on a phone's stream taps the same spot of the screen on every phone in the group, scaled to each phone's own screen size (Focus is the footer button). The View menu now sits on the Models row and only offers layouts that suit the number of devices. In plain words: tap once, all phones get the tap; no more 3-in-a-row for two phones · `12d1cc1d7`
- **Phone remote: cleaner layout** — arrow keys form a D-pad with OK in the middle and Back / Home / Menu beside it; a Type Text field with one Enter key that types the text then presses Enter (or presses Enter alone when empty); Refresh Apps is an icon next to the app list; Power and the manual rotate button are gone (orientation is detected automatically). In plain words: the phone remote is easier to read and type with · `0305ffbbc`
- **Alerts page shows today's triage and teaches the screen memory** — a line under the tiles reports today's alert-triage model calls (and how many failed), tokens, alerts discarded by a known screen without a model call, and how many screens are expected / bad / unknown (`GET /server/alerts/triageStats`). A person's Discard or Keep on an alert of a stamped screen now sets that screen's verdict (a human verdict is trusted at once and never overwritten by the model) · [TASK-84](../tasks/TASK-84-screen-memory-localize-triage.md). In plain words: you can see what triage costs today, and one click tells the memory a screen is harmless or not
- **Phone app launcher shows readable app names** — the Android phone remote's App Launcher lists one short name per app (Outlook, Notes, Netflix…), sorted A–Z, with the full package name in a tooltip on hover, instead of truncated `com.google.android.…` rows. In plain words: you can read the app list in the narrow remote panel
- **Phone remote types text and sends Enter / arrow keys** — the Android phone remote gains a key row (← ↑ ↓ → OK Enter) and a Type text field with Send; in a group-control tile both go to every phone, each with its own ✓. In plain words: type a search once and every phone in the group gets it · `ba043e73c`
- **Screen memory: the alert triage learns which screens are harmless** — a freeze alert on the host VNC desktop (device `host`, no userinterface) is now stamped by the host with the screen it showed, a fingerprint of the frame (full picture and centre, no OCR). The triage worker records each model verdict on that screen; a screen judged harmless twice, or once by a person, is trusted, and its later alerts are discarded as `fingerprint` with no model call, except every 20th sighting, which is still sent to the model as an audit. A real alert on a screen turns it `bad`. A new picture only becomes a screen after it has been seen `ALERT_SCREEN_MIN_SEEN` times (default 5), so a splash screen or a frame that drifts slightly never fills the table with unknown screens. The screen rule runs before `short`, and a not-yet-trusted screen is judged instead of skipped, so a desktop whose freezes resolve in seconds still teaches the memory. `vision.alert_triage` can be routed to the MiniMax VLM profile with an `ai_routes` row. A failed model call (for example no credits) now counts toward the daily cap and pauses model calls with a growing back-off (60 s up to 1 h) instead of retrying every 30 s, and the worker logs one line per busy cycle with calls, avoided calls, failures and tokens. Off by default: set `ALERT_SCREEN_MEMORY=dryrun` (host and server `.env`) to only log what `on` would do · [TASK-84](../tasks/TASK-84-screen-memory-localize-triage.md). In plain words: the second time the idle desktop freezes, nobody pays for the model to look at the same picture again · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261012a_screen_fingerprints.sql`
- **Group control cells: one status light, no flashing** — each stream's bottom bar now starts with a fixed-width light: a dot plus the last command's result (Sent, Error with the reason on hover, Skipped). The separate pill that switched to "Sending…" on every press is gone, so the row no longer flickers, and the cell keeps its last result until the new one arrives. The transport chip (ADB, IR, BLE) shows only when the group mixes transports. In plain words: one steady light per device that says whether the key was delivered
- **Heatmap page: clearer header and timeline** — the header is now the standard page title with the frame being shown, the All / OK / KO switch, a labelled Latest button and one gold Generate report button, followed by Devices / Active / Missing / Incidents tiles (Incidents filters to KO); the old `Frame N / 1440` counter and `Incidents detected` pill are gone. The timeline sits in its own card with previous / next buttons around a fixed-width time readout, a gold slider, one clickable segment per hour (the selected hour in the frame's status colour) and a single Yesterday | Today divider at midnight instead of repeating the day under all 24 hours. Data analysis shows its device and incident counts as pills. In plain words: the heatmap page reads like the rest of the app and the time you are looking at is obvious
- **Android app presents the owner's client certificate (app 1.0.51)** — when the deployment's host asks for a TLS client certificate (Cloudflare mTLS), the app offers Android's certificate picker once and remembers only the chosen alias; the WebView, the runtime-config and update checks, and the phone agent's Socket.IO link through the public host then present that certificate, its key read from Android's KeyChain on every handshake and never stored by the app. Other hosts asking for a certificate get none, LAN host connections are unchanged, and a declined, removed or revoked certificate clears the alias so the picker shows again. In plain words: a phone holding the owner certificate can use the app from 4G, behind an IP whitelist
- **Device page layouts show that tiles can be moved** — in a layout view (side by side, 2×2, …) a small ⋮⋮ handle with a grab cursor appears on the tile under the mouse, with the tip "Drag onto another stream to swap them"; Reset order in the View menu explains it puts the default order back. In plain words: you can now see that streams can be dragged to swap places
- **Hosts and server move to numpy 2 and OpenCV 4.13+** — `requirements.txt` now asks for `numpy>=2` and OpenCV `>=4.13` (was numpy `<2`, OpenCV `>=4.8`). OpenCV before 4.13 leaked every image file name (BUG-0474), and numpy 1.26 has no wheel for Python 3.13, the only Python on Debian 13. Checked on 210 real frames from vpt-pi1: detector, template match, histograms and edges give identical values with numpy 1.26/OpenCV 4.11 and numpy 2.4/OpenCV 4.14. Existing hosts keep their old packages until upgraded: `update_core.sh` does not run pip. Upgrade a host with `/opt/virtualpytest/venv/bin/pip install -U "numpy>=2,<3" "opencv-contrib-python>=4.13,<5"` (plus `opencv-python` to the same version where both are installed), then restart its Python services. In plain words: the platform runs on current numpy and OpenCV, and new hosts can use the system Python on Debian 13
- **Localize shows the screens it ranked, and its diagnostics move behind one click** — the popover no longer opens with a `Localized:` title and a dangling `· scope: base`; it names the interface and variant it matched against (`stb_tv · base variant`) and lists the screens the device looked like, each with its match score and a bar, the winner tinted gold. `match_fingerprint_v5` already scored every node but returned only the survivors, so it now also ships the top 8 of the full ranking (`ranked`) — `candidates`, `excluded_count`, `best_excluded` and every abstain door are untouched, `ranked[0]` is the node `best_excluded` names, and a host without the key falls back to today's single-row list. A genuine tie crowns nobody, and neither does an abstain. The per-frame classifier diagnostics (motion / freeze / loaded / overlay / playback), the closest excluded node, the matcher reason and the captured frame move to a Diagnostics dialog, because two of the three call sites are a 16–18 px icon on a preview tile. In plain words: you see which screens the device resembled and how strongly, and the "why" is one click away instead of crowding the button
- **Localize no longer contradicts the matcher's own verdict** — the popover re-thresholded confidence at 0.70 while the matcher drops candidates below `FP_MIN_CONFIDENCE` (0.55), so a node the matcher reported as a confident match could be displayed as "below the 70% threshold". The popover now trusts `candidates`, which the matcher already floored. Its verdict colours were five hardcoded hex values (one of them not even the app's success green) and three ALL-CAPS labels, both against `docs/agent/platform/UI_STYLE.md`; they now come from the agent-chat palette. In plain words: what the popover says is what the monitor says, and the popup looks like the rest of the app
- **Lite host profile for one device on a 1 GB board** — `HOST_PROFILE=lite` in the host `.env` (set before `install_host.sh`) installs only vpt-host, vpt-stream, vpt-monitor and vpt-archiver, no desktop/VNC/browser packages, and a 512 MB zram swap; on a full host it stops and disables transcript, KPI, VNC, websockify, subtitle, MediaMTX, AVQ and companion. The capture ffmpeg runs HLS only and single-threaded (vpt-pi1: 31 threads / 78 MB private → 1 thread / 51 MB, same CPU), navigation stops queuing KPI scans nobody reads, and the dashboard shows those services as optional. In plain words: a one-phone or one-box site can run on a cheap 1 GB board instead of a 4 GB Pi ([TASK-83](../tasks/TASK-83-lite-host-1gb.md))
- **Capture monitor and KPI executor use at most two malloc arenas** — their systemd units set `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2` (on the 8 reachable hosts through a `malloc-arena.conf` drop-in, already applied and restarted 2026-10-12). glibc kept freed memory in one arena per thread: 11 arenas holding 101 MB in the vpt-pi1 monitor, now 1 holding 24 MB. The monitor's total varies by more than that over hours, so expect a small gain, mostly on long-running vpt-kpi. In plain words: two background services hold on to less memory they no longer use ([TASK-83](../tasks/TASK-83-lite-host-1gb.md))
- **WebRTC can be switched off per host** — `WEBRTC_ENABLED=false` in the host `.env` stops the capture ffmpeg from encoding Opus and publishing to MediaMTX; restart `vpt-stream`. In plain words: a host that is short on CPU can stream HLS only, and the WEB button then falls back to HLS
- **WEB opens faster and comes back faster after a quality click** — on vpt-pi1 the rpitest nginx wrote one access-log line per request straight to the slow SD card, so a WHEP setup request waited 2.5–15 s behind it; the log is now buffered (POST 0.03–0.3 s; stbv1+ plays 1.9 s after the click). A LOW/SD/HD/HD+ click restarts that capture's ffmpeg: `run_ffmpeg.sh` dropped a fixed 2 s pause after the kill and its audio probe reads 0.1 s instead of 1 s, so the stream is back in 4.0–4.3 s instead of 5.8–6.2 s. vpt-mf1 gets WEB over the public IP (gateway UDP 8191), no tunnel. In plain words: the WEB picture appears in about two seconds and a quality change blacks it out for about four, not six
- **Promo videos get an automated quality gate** — `scripts/promo/promo_check.py` OCRs a built video every 0.5 s and counts error text, IPs, secrets, login forms and private names, black frames, zoom flashes, too-wide captions, length > 90 s and voice-over drift; `youtube_upload.py` refuses to upload unless all read 0 (`--skip-check` is logged), and the capture scripts share one IP/secret scrub, host-session mint and CORS relay from `promo_lib.js`. In plain words: a product video with an error or a leaked address on screen can no longer reach YouTube unnoticed
- **Device stream stays live when opening its control window** — opening a normally-live HLS or VNC preview now moves the same mounted player into the full control window instead of stopping it and creating a second connection. Playback, buffering and the VNC session continue through the transition; mute, quality changes, WEB mode and VNC interaction remain synchronized, while archive and restart modes still switch to their purpose-built players. In plain words: opening a device no longer reloads its live picture · `a46287179`
- **Host & Devices form auto-fills `DEVICEn_VIDEO_AUDIO` from the host's USB topology** — the `plughw:N,0` ALSA card used to be the only `DEVICEn_*` value the operator had to look up by hand (SSH into the host, run `arecord -l`, copy the right card number, hope the kernel doesn't reshuffle the cards on the next reboot). The Host & Devices page now shows a "Detected Grabbers" panel that calls `GET /host/system/grabbers` (proxied as `GET /server/settings/host-grabbers?host_name=...`) and lists every UVC node pre-paired with its USB UAC sibling by the same `v4l2-ctl` + `/sys` walk the operator-facing `scripts/detect_grabber.sh` does. One click on `Use` fills `DEVICEn_VIDEO` + `DEVICEn_VIDEO_AUDIO` together on the next free device, and changing the `Video Device` field also auto-fills the audio (the dynamic mapping is the source of truth — the operator-typed `.env` value is overridden whenever a detection exists, and the `.env` only remains in effect when no detection exists for that video). A small caption under the audio field tells the operator which source is in effect. Same-port pairings (the common case) are now fully automatic; cross-port pairings (HDMI audio coming from a different physical source) still work as one-shot manual overrides that get saved to `.env` but revert on the next page load. `backend_host/src/.env.example`, `setup/local/linux/backend_host/install_host.md`, and `docs/agent/devices/STREAM.md` §5 are updated to drop the "look it up by hand" guidance · [BUG-0458](../bugs/BUG-0458-2026-10-11-host-settings-audio-never-auto-filled.md)
- **WEB is its own on/off switch next to LOW / SD / HD (HD+)** — the resolution stays highlighted while WEB is on, so it is clear that WebRTC plays at that resolution (the host runs one encode for both); picking another resolution keeps WEB on. Before, WEB replaced the highlight and silently kept whatever resolution ran last, often LOW. In plain words: you can see and choose the resolution of the WEB stream
- **HD+ is back in the stream selector on hosts with hardware encode** — hosts that report VAAPI H.264 encode (`system_stats.hardware_encode`, e.g. vpt-mf1) show LOW / SD / HD / **HD+** / WEB; the Raspberry Pis keep LOW / SD / HD / WEB. HD+ is the 1280×720 @ 30 fps buffered stream. In plain words: the Minisforum gets its smooth HD+ stream back
- **WebRTC live view no longer encodes the picture twice** — `run_ffmpeg.sh` (v4l2) now sends the one HLS H.264 encode to both HLS and the MediaMTX RTSP input with ffmpeg's `tee` muxer, instead of a second 720p x264 encode for WebRTC. VAAPI hosts encode with `-bf 0` (no B-frames, needed by WebRTC; validated at 60 fps on vpt-mf1). WEB now shows the same resolution as the selected LOW/SD/HD/HD+ tier. MediaMTX down no longer stops HLS (`onfail=ignore`); restart `vpt-stream` after restarting `vpt-mediamtx` to bring WEB back. Measured on vpt-pi1: capture ffmpeg CPU LOW 35–40% → 19–22%, HD 50% → 35%, Pi load ~9 → ~5.5 (TASK-79). In plain words: the Pis spend much less CPU on live video, and HLS keeps working when the WebRTC relay is down
- **Live stream keyframes follow the frame rate above 10 fps** — `run_ffmpeg.sh` keeps a 0.4 s keyframe interval for every H.264 encode (HLS on VAAPI and libx264, and the RTSP copy for WebRTC): 8 frames at 20 fps, 24 at 60 fps, unchanged 4 at 10 fps or less. Without it a 60 fps grabber sent 15 keyframes a second and the picture blurred at the same bitrate. Taken from the 2026-10-10 vpt-mf1 60 fps validation (TASK-79). In plain words: hosts can run captures at 60 fps without a blurry stream
- **Monitoring card back in the top-left corner** — the live analysis card (time, blackscreen, freeze, macroblocks, audio, screen, motion) returns to the top-left of the stream, where it was before TASK-79 moved it top-right. In plain words: the card is where operators expect it again
- **Cloudflare Worker smart 5xx page + origin probe** — when the proxmox VM (Hetzner) is unreachable from Cloudflare, users now see a branded "Service temporarily unavailable" page (last healthy `4m ago`, the `cf-ray` request id, the upstream HTTP code, a Retry button) instead of Cloudflare's generic "Connection timed out / Error 522". A minute-by-minute cron probe in the Worker also pings `/server/health`, stores `{ok, lastOk, consecutiveFailures, alertSent}` in a CF KV namespace, and pages Slack on the operator's webhook after 2 consecutive failures with a recovery message on the way back up (no spam — sticky until ok). New code under `infra/cloudflare/` (Worker source + `wrangler.toml` + deploy README) plus the static `infra/cloudflare/custom-pages/5xx-error.html` you can paste in CF dashboard → Custom Pages → 5XX Errors as the fallback when the Worker is not bound. Deploy actions for you — `wrangler kv:namespace create STATUS_KV` + paste id into `wrangler.toml` + `wrangler secret put SLACK_WEBHOOK` + `wrangler deploy` + bind route `virtualpytest.angelstreet.io/*` to the Worker in the CF dashboard. No new server endpoint, no DB migration, no `.env` key on the proxmox VM · `974049687`
- **Group remote control (TASK-76) and device page layouts (TASK-75)** — no migration, no new `.env` key.
- Deploy **hosts as well as server and frontend**: each device now reports its `remote_profiles` (which remote and key map it has), and group control only offers devices whose host sends it. A host left on an older build shows its devices greyed out in Group control. Exact commands: `update_core.sh <branch>` (full), or `--host`, `--server` and `--frontend` one after the other.
- The device page's old page-size choice is not carried over: everyone lands on **Grid · 24 per page** once, then the View menu remembers the new choice.
- **Device page View menu: grid sizes and 9 layouts in one place** — the "Per page" select becomes a **View** menu. Its top half is the scrolling grid at 12 / 24 / 48 per page; its bottom half has layouts that fit the screen: Side by side, Stacked, 3 in a row, Grid 2×2 / 3×2 / 4×2, Focus + below, Focus + right and One full (keys 1–9 switch them). In the focus layouts the big stream plays live and the others are 1 Hz thumbnails; click a thumbnail to make it the big one. Drag a tile onto another to swap them; each layout remembers its order (Reset order in the menu). The page buttons move by the layout's slot count ("1–4 / 30 · 1 live + 3 snapshots"). The choice is kept in the browser; phones keep the grid. In plain words: watch two boxes side by side, or one big with the others beside it, without opening a stream window ([TASK-75](../tasks/TASK-75-device-page-layouts.md))
- **One remote drives several devices at once (group control)** — on the device page, **Group control** lets you pick 2–6 devices of the same model *and* the same remote (IR with the same key file, BLE with the same profile, ADB, Vega…); devices that would not react the same way are greyed out, so an stb on IR and an stb on BLE never end up together. **Control N together** opens one window with every stream and that remote: each key goes to all included devices at once, and a chip under each stream says Sent, Error or Skipped. Untick a device to leave it out, Focus it to drive it alone, Release all to give them back; a device someone else holds is listed with their name and never taken over. Atlas can do the same ("press HOME on all stbs") through the new `list_device_groups` / `execute_group_action` MCP tools and the `control-device-group` skill; the server route is `POST /server/remote/executeCommandGroup`. In plain words: drive a rack of identical boxes with one remote instead of one by one ([TASK-76](../tasks/TASK-76-group-remote-control.md))
- **Mods: add-ons you switch on, starting with Run Progress** — a new optional feature `features/mods` adds small add-ons that hook app events and draw into the app without editing pages, like Claude Code mods. Settings > Mods lists them with an on/off switch each, like browser extensions (kept per browser). The first, Run Progress, shows a bar under the top bar while scripts, campaigns and test cases run (overall %, running / passed / failed), a side pane with one row per run (host · device, step x/y, error, Open live) and a toast when a run ends. It reads the existing `/system` progress events: no new endpoint, no migration; `DISABLED_FEATURES=mods` removes it. In plain words: you can see what is running from any page
- **Blast Radius mod asks before a device is powered off or rebooted** — with the mod on (default), the Power button opens a confirm dialog naming the device and host before `power_off` or `reboot` is sent; Cancel sends nothing. Mods can now also react to device locks, host online/offline, Take Control ready/failed, deployment changes and agent activity (all from the existing `/system` updates). No new endpoint, no migration; switch it off on Settings > Mods. In plain words: one misclick no longer stops someone else's run
- **Online Users mod, mods load the current state, choices follow your account** — a new 'N online' chip in the top bar shows how many people of your team are on the platform and, on hover, who (shared logins counted apart). Mods now start from the current state when a page opens or reconnects (running scripts with their progress, held devices, who is online) instead of waiting for the next change, and each user's mod switches are saved to their account so they follow them to another computer (shared logins keep them in the browser). Server side, `/system` updates that name a team now reach only that team's browsers (and admins); fleet updates (hosts, locks) still reach everyone. In plain words: you can see who is around, and a reload no longer empties the progress bar · 🗄 DB migration `features/mods/db/001_user_mod_preferences.sql`
- **Picture-in-picture mod: keep a device's stream on every page** — the new 📺 button in the top bar picks a device; its stream floats bottom-right on every page with the usual collapse / expand / minimize. Take control from the same menu and the remote appears bottom-right (stream moves bottom-left); hide it, release or close at any time. While a page in the browser takes control of a device (Navigation Editor, Test Builder, the Rec stream window), the picture-in-picture pauses and steps back in when the page releases, so two streams never fight. No new endpoint, no migration; desktop only. In plain words: zap and watch a device while you work on another page
- **Split view mod: two pages side by side** — the new split button in the top bar (or Ctrl + Alt + S) opens a second page next to the current one, like Chrome's split view: the Device page next to the Heatmap, a run next to the Test Builder. Pick the page from the app's menu pages (recent ones first), drag the divider (double-click resets), swap sides, close with one click; it comes back as you left it after a reload. Each side works on its own like two tabs, and picture-in-picture still pauses when the second page takes control of a device. No new endpoint, no migration; desktop only; turn it off on Settings > Mods. In plain words: compare or follow two pages without switching tabs
- **Widgets on the Dashboard, starting with Team devices** — mods can now offer widgets: under the Dashboard title, "+ Add widget" lists them, and each placed widget can be moved, configured or removed from its card. The first, Team devices, shows the team's devices with tests running now and failed runs in the last 24 hours (busiest devices first, or only busy, failing or offline ones), plus offline hosts. The layout is kept in the browser; no new endpoint, no migration. In plain words: see at a glance which devices are busy or failing
- **Widgets follow your account, and Team devices picks what counts as an error** — the Dashboard widget layout (which widgets, their order and settings) is now saved to each user's account, so it follows them to another computer; a layout made before is copied up the first time (shared logins keep it in the browser). The Team devices widget gains a "Count as errors" setting: failed runs in the last 24 hours (default), failed runs in the last 7 days, or open alerts. In plain words: set up your Dashboard once, and choose whether failures or alerts are the red number · 🗄 DB migration `features/mods/db/002_user_widget_layouts.sql`
- **Targets filter gets host toggle pills** — the Device / Dashboard "All Targets" dropdown now shows a tri-state pill per host (`host-name N/M`) at the top of the menu, just below the existing Select-all / Clear row. Click an empty pill to select every device on that host; click a filled pill to deselect them all; partial selections land in the mixed state (gold dash). The pill always acts on the host as a whole — independent of the search box — so "give me everything on `vpt-pi1`" is one click. Hidden when fewer than two hosts have more than one target. No new endpoint, no new persisted state. In plain words: pick a whole rack without scrolling through devices one by one
- **Stream overlay shows live FPS and host load, and says how old it is** — in monitoring mode the device stream gains a bottom-left block: playback FPS, dropped frames and how far behind the live edge the player is, re-read every second off the video element, plus the host's CPU, RAM, 1-minute load and capture write rate with the state of `vpt-stream` and `vpt-monitor`. The two halves are labelled apart because they update on different clocks: the stream numbers are live, the host numbers come from the 60-second heartbeat, so that row carries its age and stops showing anything once the heartbeat is older than 90 s rather than leaving a healthy-looking reading next to a stream that just froze. Load average stays neutral (no core count to judge it against), unmeasurable values show '—' and never 0, and the VNC path shows only the host half because an iframe has no video to measure. No new endpoint, no extra fetch. In plain words: you can tell whether the picture froze because the host was busy or because the player stalled
- **1 Hz localize loop skips the matcher on motion, with a 1.5 s settle tail** — `monitor_localize.gate_motion_decision` short-circuits `identify_screen` on a sustained live / band / minor>1 % frame and serves a cached verdict instead, and `_update_motion_state` forces a fresh matcher call once a motion→static transition has been held for ≥ 1.5 s. The gate decision is logged on change only, plus a 30 s heartbeat (BUG-0093 shape). Tier-2 fields are absent on a gate-skip payload; a fresh run carries them. In plain words: live TV / moving carousel no longer churns a matcher answer that will only ever be 'unknown'
- **Per-frame `localize` payload carries Tier-2 (loaded / overlay / playback / error)** — `monitor_localize._screen_state` calls `shared.src.lib.ai.screen_state_cv.screen_state` on every fresh matcher run and folds the four answers into the payload; the AVQ `analyze_positions` produces one RLE lane per Tier-2 field (`positions_loaded` / `positions_overlay` / `positions_playback` / `positions_error`) alongside the existing `positions` lane. The frontend `formatScreenVerdict` surfaces them in priority order (state > error > overlay > playback > loaded > node) with new color tokens; old readers that only know the match verdict keep working. In plain words: the AVQ Frames tab and the live overlay can now say WHY a frame was hard to identify, not just THAT it was
- **Userinterface editor takes a free-text `company` label, plumbed to the monitor** — the userinterface page gains a `Company` field (placeholder `brand_a, brand_b, brand_c`), the backend persists it as a NULLABLE `userinterfaces.company` column, and the monitor plumbs it into the per-frame `localize` payload as `userinterface_company` and as a brand-aware extra keyword to the Tier-2 `error` arm (a `BRAND_A` token on a brand_a userinterface becomes a `visible` answer — a strong splash / login candidate). NULL = unknown company, treated as today. Inherits through dev→prod publish and round-trips through `.vptree` import/export. · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261008_userinterface_company.sql`
- **Userinterface editor takes an `is_media` boolean, plumbed to the Tier-2 `playback` arm** — the page gains an "Is media" column with a Switch in edit mode (a `null` on the edit form means "leave the column as-is"; flipping to a real bool persists), plus a switch in the create dialog so the editor can flag a new UI as media without leaving the row open. The backend persists it as a NULLABLE `userinterfaces.is_media` column; NULL is the safe default (caller abstains, `playback='unknown'` — same as today, byte-for-byte identical cheap 23-frame harness). TRUE / FALSE are explicit editorial choices: TRUE marks a media surface (`playback` returns `playing`/`paused`/`stopped` based on the changed_pct + audio state), FALSE marks a definitively non-media surface (`playback='not_applicable'`). Cheap verification on a moving-pct frame: `_screen_state(is_media=True)` → `playing`, `is_media=False` → `not_applicable`, `is_media=None` → `unknown`. Frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean, `ast.parse` clean on the three modified files. · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261008_userinterface_is_media.sql`
- **Tier-2 `error` arm runs a Tesseract OCR pass before `scv.error()`** — the monitor now points `pytesseract.image_to_string(..., --psm 6)` at the frame, splits the result, and hands the token list to `scv.error(tokens)`, so the arm can answer `visible` / `none` / `unknown` instead of abstaining on every frame. Lazy import (a host that only ever sees a media surface — `is_media=True` — never imports it), throttled on `is_media is not True` (a confirmed media broadcast cannot bear an error keyword, so OCR would burn cycles for no new information), and the company-aware re-check re-uses the same token stream (one pass, not two). On the cheap 23-frame harness: `error=none` P/R/F1 went 0.00/0.00/0.00 → 1.00/1.00/1.00 across the 15 knowns (no broadcast slate in the harness has an OCR-visible error keyword by design). Wall time 0.17 s → 9.7 s for the 23 frames — the price for an answer instead of an abstention. `ast.parse` clean.
- **CI/CD report cards filter the table** — on the API smoke, Frontend Component Tests and VPT Self-Test Smoke reports, clicking Passed / Failed / Probed / Skipped shows only those rows; a second click or Called/Total shows all (VPT Smoke gains the cards). pytest-html and Playwright reports already filter through their own controls; Lint, Typecheck and Web Script reports have no rows. Applies to runs from the next CI run on. In plain words: click the Failed card to see just the failures
- **Masterchief agent for elevated system operations** — the in-app AI chat (Atlas) can now propose 7 narrow system ops (read service logs, list services, check service status, restart a single service on a single host, list hosts / per-host infra summary, explain a rollout, show deploy state). The proposal renders an inline confirm card in the chat thread with the blast radius and permission; **the browser is the privilege holder** — the user clicks Confirm, the card POSTs `/server/masterchief/execute` with the user's own JWT, and the route enforces the op's required permission plus a prod-only `require_platform_admin` for mutating ops. Atlas never executes directly. Every attempt writes one row to `masterchief_audit` (RLS service-role-only, same lockdown as `agent_turns`); secret-looking keys, JWTs, PEM blocks and `k=v` patterns are stripped at the boundary, and the route layer **rejects the server `X-API-Key` outright** — this closes the `MCPAPIClient` service-key bypass documented in TASK-71 Part 1. v1 ships read-only first; `service.restart` is the single mutating op. No new API key, no `.env` change on prod; the optional `MASTERCHIEF_REQUIRE_PLATFORM_ADMIN=0` is the dev override for broader auth. 18 offline unit tests cover the op registry, redaction, route guards and rate limit. In plain words: ask Atlas to restart a service and you get a card to confirm; nothing executes without your click · [TASK-71](../tasks/TASK-71-masterchief.md) · `1228433a` (merge; feature b3479d3..a2ad476) · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261009a_masterchief_audit.sql`
- **Atlas `system-ops` skill for read-only host health checks** — when the user says "is the host up?", "how much storage is left on vpt-pi3?", "ping vpt-pi3", or "df -h", Atlas now answers directly without a confirm card. Two new MCP tools, auto-discovered as the `system_ops` category, hit two new host routes: `GET /host/system/checkStorage` (uses `shutil.disk_usage` — portable across Linux/macOS/Windows) and `GET /host/system/pingHost` (200 ms TCP cap to the host's backend port, returns RTT + boot time). The skill is narrowly scoped to read-only-host-health: every list / restart / status / log intent escalates to `propose_masterchief_op`, which is the confirm-card surface (see the matching `BUG-0450` fix below). 8 unit tests, all green. No new `.env` key, no migration, no API key; `update_core.sh <branch>` is the deploy command. In plain words: harmless host checks no longer need a click · [TASK-73](../tasks/TASK-73-atlas-testing-progressive.md) · `ebdca9ad6` (merge: de-dupe `fix/system-ops-dedupe`)

### Bug fixes
- **Product videos: no more silent voice lines** — the voice-over tool renders the voice track on its own before adding it to the video (mixing both in one ffmpeg graph dropped whole lines), writes a voice map, and fails if any line is silent; the quality gate now hears every line (`voice_silent`) and measures the decoded audio length. In plain words: a video can no longer ship with a sentence missing from its narration. [BUG-0501](../bugs/BUG-0501-2026-10-13-voiceover-drops-voice-lines.md)
- **The Tello tile can finally tell you what the drone is doing** — the status route asked the host for a page that has never existed (`/host/remote/getStatus`), so every poll 404'd. That was worse than a wrong answer: the tile aborts its whole poll on a non-2xx, so it never even reached the scan, and could only ever say "could not check" — never "AP unavailable — power on the drone", even with the drone genuinely off. The host now has a real `/host/drone/tello/status` route answering the link state, and a device with no controller answers 200 + `linked: false` rather than hiding behind another 404. In plain words: the tile reports the drone's real state instead of always failing · [BUG-0500](../bugs/BUG-0500-2026-10-13-drone-status-route-404-blocks-tile.md)
- **One unreadable alert no longer blocks the triage of all others** — when the vision model returned an empty answer for a frame, the worker paused model calls for every alert (up to an hour) and retried the same alert forever; alerts that left the 6 h window stayed untriaged. An empty or unparseable answer now only concerns that alert (skipped as `model_empty` after two tries), credit and timeout failures still pause everything, and alerts past the window are marked `expired`. Four Grafana rules got quieter: "Too many incidents on a device" no longer counts alerts the triage already settled, "Host stopped reporting" ignores the renamed `server` metrics series, "Platform service down" reads the live status table instead of the change log (it called every healthy component stale), and "Script failing repeatedly" sends one alert per device instead of one per script. In plain words: a bad frame no longer freezes alert triage, and the alert mail stops listing noise · [BUG-0499](../bugs/BUG-0499-2026-10-13-triage-blocked-by-one-empty-model-answer.md)
- **AI chat connects on its own when the key is there** — the "API key needed" popup re-checks the server every 15 s instead of staying up until a reload, the browser no longer autofills the key field, and the popup is shorter (title, one line, a settings icon, Save). In plain words: the chat no longer asks for a key it already has · [BUG-0498](../bugs/BUG-0498-2026-10-13-ai-chat-key-popup-stuck-after-one-no-key-answer.md)
- **AI usage: the Training panel loads again** — it listed samples without their id and failed with a bare `'id'`. In plain words: the training data panel shows its numbers · [BUG-0495](../bugs/BUG-0495-2026-10-12-ai-usage-training-panel-keyerror-id.md)
- **AI usage: host features are no longer counted as external API** — localize second opinion, triage and other host-side calls now show origin Monitoring. In plain words: the "API" share is only real outside callers · [BUG-0496](../bugs/BUG-0496-2026-10-12-ai-usage-host-calls-attributed-to-api.md)
- **A provider with no credit is checked for free, and background AI calls wait** — the pause now ends by reading the account's credit instead of sending a paid request that fails; Jev shadow features are skipped (and counted) while their provider is paused. In plain words: no more paying to learn there is still no credit · [BUG-0497](../bugs/BUG-0497-2026-10-12-ai-no-credit-pause-paid-probe-and-shadow-calls.md)
- **Localize crowns the screen the matcher actually chose** — on a menu the popover could gold-tint a screen the title had rejected; a tie no longer shows a winner, an abstain says why (e.g. a near-empty frame), and step 2 shows the screen state (motion, loaded, overlay, playback, error). In plain words: the popover now says the same thing the matcher decided · [BUG-0494](../bugs/BUG-0494-2026-10-12-localize-popover-crowned-ranked-not-verdict.md) · `2289edffe`
- **Tello pairing works on real hardware** — the host-side drone routes tested the `(value, error)` tuple that `require_field` returns, and a non-empty tuple is always truthy, so `/host/drone/tello/pair`, `mission_pads`, `flight_summary` and `last_known` all answered `400 {"error": ["<device_id>", null]}` before reading a single field; on top of that `'tello'` was missing from `controller_class_patterns` (so the lookup returned `None` even though the controller is built), and the AP join shelled out to `sudo nmcli`, which `vpt_user` cannot do (polkit refuses; it is not in the `network` group). All three are fixed: the routes unpack the tuple like every other host route, `tello` resolves to `TelloRemoteController`, `nmcli` is tried unprivileged before `sudo -n`, an already-associated host is a no-op rather than a failure, and the SDK link check — not the join — decides the outcome. A new `tello-wifi` sudoers drop-in grants `vpt_user` only `device wifi rescan` and `device wifi connect TELLO-*`. In plain words: pairing a real drone no longer fails with an error that names nothing · [BUG-0490](../bugs/BUG-0490-2026-10-12-tello-pair-routes-400-and-missing-controller.md)
- **Phone remote Scr↓ scrolls the page down** — the Scr buttons sent the swipe the arrow points, and a finger swiping down drags a page toward its top, so Scr↓ did nothing on a page at its top. They now send the opposite finger swipe, sized to the phone's live screen (70%→30% of its height) instead of fixed pixels. In plain words: Scr↓ on a phone, or on a group of phones, now shows what is further down the page · [BUG-0489](../bugs/BUG-0489-2026-10-12-phone-remote-scroll-buttons-scroll-the-wrong-way.md)
- **Every alert carries a frame** — a freeze with no thumbnail on disk now uploads its capture frames, and an alert whose frame did not upload is not created: it stays pending and the upload is retried every minute. In plain words: no more alerts with an empty picture that nobody (and no AI) can judge · [BUG-0493](../bugs/BUG-0493-2026-10-12-alerts-created-without-a-frame.md) · `10de3cb4c`
- **A model account with no credit is no longer called on every request** — a 402 from a model provider now pauses that endpoint for every AI usage in the process (2 min, doubling to 1 h), with one probe call per pause; alert triage no longer sends alerts without a frame to the image-only vision model, and the Alerts page shows "Triage unavailable: <reason>" while the triage model fails. In plain words: an empty OpenRouter account costs a few failed calls an hour instead of thousands, and the Alerts page tells you why alerts stay unchecked · [BUG-0492](../bugs/BUG-0492-2026-10-12-ai-no-credit-402-retried-on-every-call.md) · `fa248686b`
- **Screenshot-streamed phones follow the phone again** — a phone streamed by adb screenshots (no HDMI capture) showed every change 3-4 s late: ffmpeg decoded its PNG frames on ~20 threads, each holding a frame, and stamped them on a clock running at half speed. It now decodes on one thread and stamps frames with the real time. In plain words: in group control the screenshot phone's tile now shows a key press about as fast as the others · [BUG-0491](../bugs/BUG-0491-2026-10-12-screencap-phone-stream-lags-seconds-behind.md)
- **A camera or HDMI device no longer plays a stale video file or URL** — runtime source switches (`active_video_path.conf` / `active_rtsp_url.conf`) are keyed by capture dir and outlive `.env` edits, so a capture dir reassigned from a video-file device to a camera kept streaming the old file. vpt-stream now applies a switch only to the device models that make one (video file → `local_video`, URL → `rtsp_video` / `tello`). In plain words: changing which device uses a capture folder can no longer leave an old film playing in its tile · [BUG-0488](../bugs/BUG-0488-2026-10-12-stale-source-override-replaces-camera.md)
- **Every billed AI call is counted** — chat history compaction, public Ask, the MCP prompt, Settings Test buttons, browser-use on host GPUs and each retry of an empty answer now appear in the AI stats, with cache, reasoning and web-search usage and provider cost kept · [BUG-0486](../bugs/BUG-0486-2026-10-12-billed-ai-calls-not-counted.md) · `ee7dde051`
- 🚨 **CRITICAL — hotfix every BLE robot with two or more remotes: Resume on one STB could kill the other STB's remote** — a Resume on one STB could give its dongle the other dongle's address (after a stale adapter-hciN.mac binding), and the remote daemon then ran on the wrong dongle; both STBs lost their remote and one STB dropped its pairing key (needs a Re-pair). Fixed: Resume keeps an adapter on its own bonded address and refuses any address another adapter uses; a remote daemon only runs on its own adapter. In plain words: resuming one STB's remote can no longer knock out the other STB's remote on the same robot. Hotfix = replace `backend_host/src/controllers/remote/bluetooth/resume.sh` + `hid_remote.py` on the host (no restart needed); check `timeout 5 hciconfig hciN` shows a different `BD Address` per adapter · [BUG-0483](../bugs/BUG-0483-2026-10-12-ble-resume-steals-sibling-adapter-mac.md)
- **Changing stream quality no longer hangs on "Loading HD quality stream..."** — once the new stream was ready the player skipped its reload (same URL, still marked loaded) and waited for the old stream to fail, then 6 s more; and while the switch ran it kept fetching deleted segments until it gave up as "FFmpeg stuck" for good. It now stops fetching during the switch and reloads as soon as the new stream is ready. In plain words: LOW / SD / HD switches take a few seconds again · [BUG-0485](../bugs/BUG-0485-2026-10-12-quality-switch-overlay-stuck.md) · `418feeaf6`
- **Group control: every phone fills its tile** — a phone that streams a 16:9 frame with black side bars (the S21) was shown as a small picture in the middle of its group tile. Phone tiles now crop to the screen like Take Control does. In plain words: in a phone group, the S21 is as big as the S7 · [BUG-0484](../bugs/BUG-0484-2026-10-12-group-control-s21-stream-shrunk.md) · `632f021c9`
- **Script triage stops calling an AI provider that is down** — after a failed call (no credits, outage) Sherlock's Jev step and LLM step each pause 1 h, then 5 h, then 24 h, and resume on the first success; the LLM step now runs on MiniMax M2.7 highspeed. In plain words: an empty OpenRouter account no longer turns every script result into an AI error · [BUG-0482](../bugs/BUG-0482-2026-10-12-script-triage-retries-a-down-provider.md)
- **Phone tap overlay fits every phone, not just the S21** — the overlay over the phone stream assumed a 1080x2340 screen, so on a Galaxy S7 (1440x2560) its boxes were squeezed and missed the icons. It now uses the screen size the phone reports. In plain words: the clickable boxes line up on any phone · [BUG-0481](../bugs/BUG-0481-2026-10-12-phone-overlay-wrong-size-on-s7.md) · `f9d9ff9b5`
- **Settings page no longer loads twice** — opening Configuration → Settings showed the spinner, the page, then the spinner again while the first host's config loaded. In plain words: Settings opens once, without the flicker · [BUG-0480](../bugs/BUG-0480-2026-10-12-settings-page-loads-twice.md) · `a294f0c34`
- **Group control of phones: the remote's buttons work again** — the phone remote's tap overlay was drawn over the group tile's remote panel, so its clicks tapped the first phone instead (App Launcher stuck at 0 apps, stray apps opening). The group tile no longer draws that overlay. In plain words: in a phone group, the buttons on the right do what they say · [BUG-0479](../bugs/BUG-0479-2026-10-12-group-phone-remote-clicks-tap-the-phone.md) · `06c3a6ab2`
- **A Windows host running Docker now says so on its dashboard card** — the host card printed `platform.system()` verbatim, so `vpt-windows` — a Windows PC whose vpt-host runs as a Linux Docker container (confirmed: `checkStorage` returns the container id `8e20da5d30d8` and a POSIX `/` mount, and all 680 recorded samples say `Linux`) — was labelled "Linux (x86_64)", with the giveaway stats that a Linux box cannot fake being absent on a real one: a live load average and a 2%-full 1 TB root. The host now also reports what it is running *inside* and what machine it sits on (`in_container`, `container_runtime`, `is_wsl`, `physical_platform`, `os_mismatch`), and the card shows `Docker · Windows (x86_64)` with a tooltip explaining the mismatch. The physical OS is only ever claimed on positive evidence of the WSL2 kernel — a host *name* is never used to infer an OS, which is what let the mismatch go unnoticed. The fields ride the existing host ping, so there is no DB migration; bare-metal hosts render exactly as before. In plain words: the card tells you the box is a Windows PC that happens to be running vpt-host in Docker, instead of claiming it is a Linux machine · [BUG-0478](../bugs/BUG-0478-2026-10-12-dashboard-shows-windows-host-as-linux-x86-64.md)
- **The script-execute API now refuses targets a script does not allow** — `POST /server/script/execute` applies the same `_target_rules` as the web app: a host-only script is refused on a device or on a host with no host target, a device script on `host`, and wrong host OS or device model, with a 400 naming the reason. Scripts without `_target_rules` are unchanged. In plain words: an API call can no longer run a script where the app would not let you [BUG-0477](../bugs/BUG-0477-2026-10-12-script-execute-api-ignores-target-rules.md)
- **Localize's captured-frame button opens the capture instead of a 401** — `/host/<name>/stream/...` sits behind the proxy's host-session cookie, which only a player or a failed `<img>` mints; the button was a plain link, so the new tab loaded unauthenticated. It now goes through `openHostUrl()`, which mints the cookie and then opens · [BUG-0487](../bugs/BUG-0487-2026-10-12-localize-view-captured-frame-401.md)
- **Localize stops second-guessing the matcher's verdict** — the popover re-thresholded confidence at 0.70 while the matcher drops candidates below `FP_MIN_CONFIDENCE` (0.55), so between the two a screen the matcher accepted could be shown as "below the threshold"; the monitor, `verify_destination` and the popover disagreed about the same frame. The popover now reads `candidates` (the matcher's own answer), a genuine tie crowns neither row, and an abstain crowns none. Its verdict colours were five hardcoded hex values (one not even the app's success green) and three ALL-CAPS labels, both against `docs/agent/platform/UI_STYLE.md`; they now come from the agent-chat palette · [BUG-0476](../bugs/BUG-0476-2026-10-12-localize-popover-threshold-contradicts-matcher.md)
- **`/server/health` answers within ~2 s even when Redis is down** — the health check pinged Redis with the queue client's 5 s timeout and redis-py's 3 automatic retries, so while the Redis VM was unreachable one call took 12-27 s and the Cloudflare status probe reported the server down. The health ping is now one attempt with a 2 s cap; queue operations are unchanged. In plain words: the uptime monitor no longer mistakes a Redis outage for the whole server being down · [BUG-0475](../bugs/BUG-0475-2026-10-12-server-health-slow-when-redis-unreachable.md)
- **Capture monitor no longer grows until the host runs out of memory** — OpenCV kept every image file name the monitor read (two per frame), so it grew about 0.6 GB a day (1.45 GB on vpt-pi1, 3.4 GB on vpt-nipogi). Images are now read and written through `cv2_io`, which does not leak. In plain words: the monitor stays the same size however long it runs, so small hosts stop filling their memory · [BUG-0474](../bugs/BUG-0474-2026-10-12-capture-monitor-grows-cv2-path-string-leak.md)
- **Host VNC previews are centred in their tile again** — a `host_vnc` device on `/device-control` (vpt-mf1, labox-web, vpt-pi1/3) drew its remote desktop flush against the top-left of the tile, with ~175 px of dead black space to the right, which made a working preview look broken. This was a side effect of the persistent-player work (BUG-0465 / BUG-0470), not of the earlier sizing fix: the player is portal-rendered into a `persistentStreamRoot` div that gets re-parented between the preview and the modal, and that re-parenting moved the sized wrapper out of the stream area's flex container. Because the slot it lands in is `position: absolute`, it stopped being a flex item, so the area's `alignItems`/`justifyContent` silently became dead CSS and the wrapper fell back to its static top-left position. Sizing was never wrong — `vncScaledSize` still fits the 1440×847 desktop into the measured area — so the picture was the right size and aspect, just parked in the wrong corner. The wrapper now centres itself against the portal root (`top/left: 50%` + `translate(-50%,-50%)`), which holds wherever the root is attached, so the preview and the modal can no longer drift apart. Restart nothing; the frontend rebuild covers it. In plain words: the desktop sits in the middle of its tile again, at every tile size · [BUG-0473](../bugs/BUG-0473-2026-10-11-host-vnc-preview-not-centred.md) — BUG-0466 gave every tile the same width, which evened out the gaps but left all three lines of each tile (uppercase label, big number, caption) hard against its column's left edge, with a void to the right that grew with the tile. A `grow` tile now sets `text-align: center`, so label, value and caption share one vertical axis in every column — the measured offset of each line from its tile's centre is 0px for all fifteen. The grid is unchanged (122px columns, uniform 32px gaps). `grow` is opt-in and only the headline row passes it, so the other `StatTile` rows on Run Summary, Share Run, Run Group Detail and the Alerts tab keep their current left alignment. In plain words: the headline row now reads as one centred row · [BUG-0471](../bugs/BUG-0471-2026-10-11-analytics-kpi-tile-content-not-centered.md)
- **The five KPI tiles on `/monitoring/analytics` now centre their own content** — BUG-0466 gave every tile the same width, which evened out the gaps but left all three lines of each tile (uppercase label, big number, caption) hard against its column's left edge, with a void to the right that grew with the tile. A `grow` tile now sets `text-align: center`, so label, value and caption share one vertical axis in every column — the measured offset of each line from its tile's centre is 0px for all fifteen. The grid is unchanged (122px columns, uniform 32px gaps). `grow` is opt-in and only the headline row passes it, so the other `StatTile` rows on Run Summary, Share Run, Run Group Detail and the Alerts tab keep their current left alignment. In plain words: the headline row now reads as one centred row · [BUG-0471](../bugs/BUG-0471-2026-10-11-analytics-kpi-tile-content-not-centered.md)
- **Hovering a snapshot tile on Device Control shows the live stream again** — with more tiles than the live limit, tiles show the last image and hovering one should swap it to live video, but the tile went black: the live player is drawn into a detached box that is moved into the tile's slot, and that slot only appears on hover, which never triggered the move. The move now re-runs when the tile switches between snapshot and live. In plain words: hover a tile and you see it live instead of a black box · [BUG-0470](../bugs/BUG-0470-2026-10-11-rec-hover-live-blackscreen.md)
- **Report videos no longer lose their last minute now and then** — building the video read the archive first and the live segments seconds later, and the archiver could archive and delete those live segments in between, so the end of the test was in neither (43 s lost on vpt-pi1). The live segments are now pinned before the archive is read, and the archiver deletes a minute of live segments only after it is in the archive. In plain words: a report video now covers the whole test even when it is built while the archiver is busy · [BUG-0469](../bugs/BUG-0469-2026-10-11-report-video-loses-tail-when-archiver-rotates-hot.md)
- **The status worker no longer runs out of free Cloudflare KV quota** — the minute-by-minute health probe saved its state to KV on every tick (1,440 writes/day against a free limit of 1,000), so from mid-afternoon UTC each day the monitor stopped saving state and could not send DOWN / RECOVERED emails; and every site request read KV with no error handling, so hitting the read limit would have failed the whole site. The probe now writes only when the state changes plus an hourly heartbeat (~24 writes/day when healthy), and a KV error falls back to "unknown" instead of blocking traffic. Redeploy with `npx wrangler deploy` in `infra/cloudflare/status-worker`. In plain words: the outage monitor stays on all day and can never take the site down itself · [BUG-0468](../bugs/BUG-0468-2026-10-11-status-worker-kv-daily-write-quota.md)
- **Rec preview tile is no longer black on first page mount** — since the persistent-stream change (the player is rendered into a detached `<div>` that a `useLayoutEffect` is supposed to move into the per-tile slot), the effect's deps never changed when `useStream` resolved and the slot finally appeared, so the orphan `<div>` stayed detached, `hls.js` happily attached media and parsed the manifest against a `<video>` that was never in the document tree, and every tile read as black until the operator opened and closed a modal on it. Adding `streamUrl` to the effect's deps re-runs it the moment the slot is created; the body is idempotent, so the extra run is free. In plain words: a fresh load of `/device-control` paints the streams instead of staying black · [BUG-0465](../bugs/BUG-0465-2026-10-11-rec-preview-tile-blackscreen-on-first-mount.md)
- **Dashboard no longer crashes on load** — a host carrying only the lightweight `system_stats` (platform / HD+ / 24h-archive flags, no CPU-RAM-disk) made the host card call `.toFixed` on nothing; the card now waits for the real numbers. In plain words: the Dashboard opens again instead of showing the crash page · [BUG-0467](../bugs/BUG-0467-2026-10-11-dashboard-crash-partial-system-stats.md)
- **The five KPI tiles at the top of `/monitoring/analytics` line up on one grid** — Robots, Devices, Open incidents, Active alerts and Pass rate each carried a hand-picked `minWidth`, so a tile was as wide as its own text and any surplus stayed *inside* it as trailing whitespace: `Devices` reserved 150px for a 114px number and opened a 36px hole after itself. On top of the fixed 32px gap that made the four gaps between tiles read 35 / 67 / 37 / 46px instead of one value. The tiles now take an equal share of the row (`flex: 1 1 0`, an opt-in `grow` prop on `StatTile`) and share one 120px floor sized to the widest label, so all five are 122px with a uniform 32px gap and wrap evenly on a narrow window. The numbers themselves were never mis-sized — all five already shared one 30px baseline, and the `164`/`6` looking smaller than `12 / 13` is just the slash raising that row's cap line. In plain words: the headline row reads as one aligned row instead of five loosely spaced ones · [BUG-0466](../bugs/BUG-0466-2026-10-11-analytics-kpi-tiles-not-aligned.md)
- **The host device's screenshot and archive work again on a host whose VNC runs under a different home** — on vpt-mf1 the host capture (`HOST_VIDEO_SOURCE=:1`, the only grabber that reads the VNC desktop over X11) produced nothing: empty capture folder, no video, and `takeScreenshot` answering 500 in the browser. `vpt-vnc.service` runs TigerVNC with `HOME=/var/lib/vpt_user`, so the display's X cookie is written to `/var/lib/vpt_user/.Xauthority`; `vpt-stream.service` runs `run_ffmpeg.sh` as the same `vpt_user` but without that override, so systemd gave it `/home/vpt_user`, where no `.Xauthority` exists. ffmpeg died on startup with `Authorization required, but no authorization protocol specified` / `Cannot open display :1`, and the staleness watchdog dutifully restarted it every 60 s — which is why the host looked live while archiving nothing. (The archiver and `vpt-monitor` were both healthy: the monitor's `freeze` verdict was an accurate report, not a fault.) `run_ffmpeg.sh` now resolves the cookie itself through a new `setup_x11_auth()` — it prefers an already-exported `XAUTHORITY`, then asks the live X server for its own `-auth` path, then falls back to the VNC home and `$HOME`. Asking the X server keeps this correct if the VNC home ever moves, instead of hard-coding a path. The same cookie also fixes the `xdpyinfo` resolution probe, which had been silently falling back to 1280x720. Restart `vpt-stream` after deploying. In plain words: the host's picture and its recordings come back · [BUG-0464](../bugs/BUG-0464-2026-10-11-host-x11grab-xauthority-missing.md)
- **The Pi's SD card no longer stalls under the archiver** — each minute the archiver re-wrote the whole open 10-minute video chunk on the SD card (~110x a minute of video per chunk, ~2 MB/s per HD device), and the card stalled for seconds. The open chunk is now built in RAM and written to the card once when it is complete; the 1-minute MP4 stays in RAM, the MP3 is appended in place. New host `.env` key `ARCHIVE_VIDEO=false` turns the 24h review buffer off entirely (the Last 24h button is then disabled). In plain words: the Pi writes about ten times less video to its SD card, and a host can skip the 24h replay altogether · [BUG-0463](../bugs/BUG-0463-2026-10-11-archiver-rewrites-10min-chunk-on-sd-every-minute.md)
- **Device Control: unselected chip in the Group Models strip reads as page-text in dark mode too** — BUG-0461 fixed the selected branch (gold-on-gold = invisible). The unselected branch — gold-accent text on a 13% gold tint over the dark bar surface — was at ~4.48:1, right on the WCAG AA edge and visibly faded. The unselected branch now uses `P.textPrimary` (`#f0f0f0` in dark, `#141414` in light), keeping the gold tint so the chip still reads as a gold-accented filter; 9.5:1 in dark, 7.1:1 in light. Selected (`'#1a1a1a'` on full gold), disabled (`P.textMuted`) and hover branches are unchanged — all three states now pass WCAG AA comfortably. In plain words: every chip in the Models strip is readable, whether it's in the group or not · [BUG-0462](../bugs/BUG-0462-2026-10-11-group-chip-unselected-label-dim-dark-mode.md)
- **Device Control: selected chip in the Group Models strip is no longer blank in dark mode** — once a model had at least one device in the group, its chip filled with the gold accent (`#d4a574`) and its label was painted with `P.accentText`, which is **also** `#d4a574` in the dark palette (AGENT_CHAT_PALETTE), so the model name vanished inside its own background and the operator could no longer tell which model was selected. The selected branch now uses a fixed `#1a1a1a` — the same dark-on-gold convention every other gold-fill button in the app already follows (PageChrome `goldButtonSx`, RunTests / BuildCampaigns primary buttons, Masterchief confirm card, TestCaseBuilder toggle); 7:1 contrast on the gold, both modes, no new palette token. The unselected and disabled branches are unchanged. In plain words: the model you just added to the group is readable again · [BUG-0461](../bugs/BUG-0461-2026-10-11-group-chip-selected-label-invisible-dark-mode.md)
- **Mods no longer log two 500s on every page load** — `/server/mods/preferences` and `/server/mods/widgets` answered 500 on any site whose database never had the mods tables (`Could not find the table 'public.user_mod_preferences' in the schema cache`, `PGRST205`), because `features/mods/db/001` and `002` are applied by hand on every database a release reaches. The app still worked — it kept the choices and the widget layout in the browser — but the same failure hit the `PUT` paths, so switching a mod on or off and reordering widgets were silently never saved. Both tables were applied on the database behind rpitest (the shared Supabase at `virtualpytest.angelstreet.io/supabase`) and its PostgREST schema cache reloaded, and the server now answers an empty result instead of a 500 when those tables are absent — so a site that forgets a migration keeps working quietly instead of logging two errors per page load. Real errors (`PGRST200`, timeouts) are still reported. In plain words: your mod switches and Dashboard widget layout now really follow your account · [BUG-0460](../bugs/BUG-0460-2026-10-11-mods-tables-not-migrated-500-every-page-load.md)
- **Phone/emulator live view survives a stream restart** — the image-file grabber (devices fed from `emulator_frames/.../latest.png`) was the only one without `-y`, so after a `vpt-stream` restart ffmpeg found `thumbnails/latest.jpg` already there and exited; the device stayed black. In plain words: restarting streaming no longer kills the phone pictures ([BUG-0457](../bugs/BUG-0457-2026-10-11-imagefile-grabber-exits-latest-jpg-exists.md))
- **DOM report opens in a new tab even when the selected server is a sibling origin** — opening the report on `rpitest.angelstreet.io` from the main UI at `virtualpytest.angelstreet.io` used to land on `{"error":"Authentication required", …}` because `window.open` cannot carry the JWT and the mirrored `vpt_jwt` cookie is host-only. The Interface page now calls a new `GET /server/userinterface/domReport/url` (the same script-reports / `openR2Url` pattern), which builds (or re-signs) the cached R2 artifact at `navigation/<safe>/dom_report.html` and returns a presigned URL against the public proxy. The new tab now lands on `virtualpytest.angelstreet.io/virtualpytest/.../dom_report.html?X-Amz-…` — no Authorization header, no cookie, no cross-origin — and the URL stays valid for 7 days. The existing HTML route and its test are unchanged for curl / internal callers · [BUG-0459](../bugs/BUG-0459-2026-10-11-dom-report-new-tab-401-when-selected-server-is-sibling-origin.md)
- **DOM report signed URL follows the same `team_id` convention as the rest of `/server/*`** — the BUG-0459 helper called `api.get` with a hand-built path, but `apiClient` does not auto-append `team_id` (only `buildServerUrl` does). The new tab 400ed on `userinterface and team_id are required`. The path is now wrapped in `buildServerUrl` like every other SPA → server call, so the request carries `team_id=…` like the route expects · [BUG-0472](../bugs/BUG-0472-2026-10-11-dom-report-signed-url-missing-team-id-400.md)
- **Host & Devices form auto-fills DEVICE_VIDEO_AUDIO from the host's USB topology** — registering a device used to require SSHing into the host to copy the ALSA card number (`plughw:2,0` today, `plughw:4,0` after the next reboot); a new "Detected Grabbers" panel above the device list calls `GET /host/system/grabbers` and shows every UVC node already paired with its USB UAC sibling. One click on `Use` fills `DEVICE_VIDEO` + `DEVICE_VIDEO_AUDIO` together on the next free device, and changing the `Video Device` field also auto-fills the audio (the dynamic mapping is now the source of truth — the operator-typed .env value is overridden by detection whenever a match exists, and the .env remains in effect only when no detection exists for that video). A small caption under the audio field tells the operator whether the value is "auto-detected from USB 3-1" or "from .env". Cross-port pairings (HDMI audio coming from a different physical source) still work as one-shot manual overrides that get saved to .env but revert on the next page load — the common same-port case is now fully automatic. The empty case points the operator at `setup/local/linux/backend_host/setup_permissions.sh` (the host service user must be in the `video`/`audio` groups) · [BUG-0458](../bugs/BUG-0458-2026-10-11-host-settings-audio-never-auto-filled.md)
- **WEB view keeps the Monitoring card and has sound** — choosing WEB used to drop the Monitoring card (it lived inside the HLS player) and the WebRTC stream carried no audio (video only, and the player was always muted). The card now sits over both players, the host sends an Opus audio track to WebRTC alongside the AAC one for HLS, and WEB follows the speaker button. Needs the host deploy for the sound (`run_ffmpeg.sh`, restarts `vpt-stream`). In plain words: WEB looks and sounds like the other qualities, only faster · [BUG-0456](../bugs/BUG-0456-2026-10-11-webrtc-no-monitoring-overlay-no-audio.md)
- **Device Control: status chip on REC preview cards shrinks to a coloured dot** — the bottom-aligned "online" / "running" / "error" text in the preview-card header crowded out the Localize button and any feature link (e.g. AVQ Audio/Video Quality) on cards with long device names or multiple flag chips. The chip is now a 10×10 coloured dot (red for offline / stuck services, blue while a deployment runs, green for healthy); the full status text is carried in a hover tooltip that mirrors the wording in the stream-modal toolbar, so the same host reads the same way in the grid and in the modal. Same 24×20 footprint, so the header geometry is stable and Localize + AVQ fit again. In plain words: the Localize and AVQ buttons are visible at last, and the dot still tells you whether the host is alive · [BUG-0455](../bugs/BUG-0455-2026-10-11-rechostpreview-status-chip-crowds-buttons.md)
- **WEB plays in the app, not only in the test page** — the host's WebRTC server answered every site with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, which browsers refuse when the request carries the login cookie, so the app's WEB request was blocked before it started. vpt-pi1 now answers only the platform's own sites, by name; the test page now sends the cookie like the app. Hand-edit on each MediaMTX host (`/opt/mediamtx/mediamtx.yml`, `webrtcAllowOrigins`, see docs/agent/devices/WEBRTC.md §1). In plain words: clicking WEB on vpt-pi1 really switches to the low-latency stream · [BUG-0454](../bugs/BUG-0454-2026-10-11-whep-cors-wildcard-blocks-credentials.md)
- **WEB shows its loading screen on the click and plays in under a second** — "Loading WebRTC stream..." now appears as soon as WEB is chosen (it used to wait for a cookie request first), and the browser sends its offer as soon as it knows its public address instead of always waiting 1 s. On STB4 / vpt-pi1 from outside the LAN the picture starts at 0.75 s instead of 1.8 s. In plain words: WEB reacts at once and the live picture comes faster · [BUG-0453](../bugs/BUG-0453-2026-10-11-web-loading-overlay-late.md)
- **WEB (WebRTC) stream no longer falls back to HLS after 6 s** — the browser waited for ICE gathering to finish before sending its offer, and in Chrome that can take 20 s+ (a STUN check on an interface that never answers), so the 6 s connect timeout always won. The offer now goes out after at most 1 s with the candidates found so far; from an outside network the picture plays in about 2 s. In plain words: the WEB button shows the low-latency stream instead of quietly switching back to HLS · [BUG-0449](../bugs/BUG-0449-2026-10-11-whep-waits-for-full-ice-gathering.md)
- **Dashboard: per-server CPU / RAM / Disk / °C / Load chips now sit inside the "Server Services" header (right of the "online" chip)** — the URL row already carried URL + version + the five resource chips + host/device chips + two icon buttons, so the chips were eating row space and pushing the controls. They moved into the `<AccordionSummary>` of the collapsible header: title + "online" chip on the left, resource chips on the right (expand chevron stays on the far right). Same colour treatment, same data, only the location changed. In plain words: the chips are no longer fighting the URL row for space · [BUG-0448](../bugs/BUG-0448-2026-10-09-server-card-blank-cpu-ram-on-servers-with-SERVER-NAME.md)
