- **Dashboard: per-server resource chips now show on every deployment that sets `SERVER_NAME`** — the gunicorn metrics collector stored rows with a hardcoded `host_name='server'` while `getAllHosts?include_system_stats=true` read them with `os.getenv('SERVER_NAME', 'server')`. Every deployment that sets `SERVER_NAME` to anything but the literal `server` (which is every `.env` template in the repo: `hetzner1`, `vpt-docker`, `Awesomation`, …) wrote metrics that no read could ever find, so the chips stayed blank on that card. The store now keys by the same env var the read uses (`(os.getenv('SERVER_NAME') or '').strip() or 'server'`, matching the rest of the backend), and a code comment points future readers at the read side so the two never drift again. Live within one minute of worker boot (the collector's first tick). In plain words: the SSH server card on `virtualpytest.angelstreet.io` now shows its own CPU/RAM/Disk/°C/Load, not a blank gap · [BUG-0448](../bugs/BUG-0448-2026-10-09-server-card-blank-cpu-ram-on-servers-with-SERVER-NAME.md)
- **Device Control: host_vnc preview now fills any large tile (One full, focus layouts)** — the tile's VNC iframe was pinned to a hardcoded 300×150 wrapper, so in the "One full" view (and the focus layouts) a `host_vnc` card sat as a tiny 255×150 desktop centred in a huge black area. `RecHostPreview` now mirrors the modal's `ResizeObserver` pattern: the wrapper box is measured on every layout / window / pagination change and the iframe scaling is recomputed from that size, preserving the 1440×847 desktop aspect ratio. In plain words: `vpt-pi3` in "One full" now shows the full desktop instead of a thumbnail in a black card · [BUG-0447](../bugs/BUG-0447-2026-10-09-vnc-tile-fill.md)
- **Device Control: Targets host-toggle pills now follow the in-dropdown search** — the new host pill row at the top of the "All Targets" menu used the full target list, so typing `mf1` to find `vpt-mf1` still showed the `vpt-pi1` and `vpt-pi3` pills alongside it. `hostGroups` still groups from the full `targetOptions` (so a pill click still toggles the whole host rack), but the displayed pills are now narrowed to hosts that have at least one visible target — matching the targets list below them. Clearing the search restores the full pill row. In plain words: when you narrow the list, the pills narrow with it · [BUG-0446](../bugs/BUG-0446-2026-10-09-device-control-targets-pills-ignore-search.md)
- **Device Control grid cards are the same height in every row again** — the BUG-0439 fix that unified the grid and layout tiles under one CSS-grid stage also broke `align-items: stretch` for the grid branch: a non-mobile tile's `minHeight: 300px` + `aspectRatio: 16/9` settled at 300 px while a mobile tile settled at ~ 820 px (its natural 9:16 height), so a row of three devices no longer lined up. We reverted BUG-0439 — the page now goes back to the MUI `Grid item xs/sm/md/lg = 12/6/4/3` branch for the grid (every card matches the tallest in the row) and keeps `<DeviceLayout>` for the 9 fixed layouts. The trade-off is the original BUG-0439 symptom: switching between a grid size and a layout (or back) tears down every visible HLS player and re-handshakes the `.m3u8`, so the cards flash to black for a beat. That bug is now re-opened and tracked on the BUG-0451 file. In plain words: the grid is back to a tidy same-height row, and the streams-reload-on-view-change is back too · [BUG-0451](../bugs/BUG-0451-2026-10-09-device-control-grid-cards-different-heights.md)
- **Device Control: pagination no longer overlays the bottom row of preview tiles** — at 12 / 24 / 48 per page the page footer's `« ‹ 1 2 3 › »` row sat on top of the last row of cards (the same `aspect-ratio: 16/9` lock from BUG-0439 that made the cards line up also made the inner `<Card>` 180 px tall, which is shorter than the aspect-ratio row at the breakpoints where the pager overlapped). The pager is now a sticky 56 px footer with its own reserved space, and the grid no longer tries to render one row under it. In plain words: the bottom row of previews is no longer hidden under the pager · [BUG-0452](../bugs/BUG-0452-2026-10-11-device-control-pagination-overlays-grid.md)
- **Group control: Release all (and Esc) also exit the group** — clicking **Release all** in the group modal (or pressing Esc / browser back) used to close the modal and release the devices, but the page stayed in group mode with the previous selection still highlighted, so reopening the group bar on the same model showed the same devices still picked. The modal's `handleRelease` now calls a new `onExitGroup` prop after `onClose()`, and `Rec.tsx` passes `groupSelection.toggleGroupMode` — which already clears `selected` and `remoteProfile` when toggling off. Both close paths land back on the normal Edit / Group control + filters row with a clean selection. In plain words: leaving the group modal leaves group mode too · [BUG-0442](../bugs/BUG-0442-2026-10-09-group-control-release-all-exits-group.md)
- **Edit / Group control / Exit group are now the same style, and the selection count flashes on change** — the `Group control` button used to be plain gold text in the off state and a gold-outlined button in the on state; the border flashed in/out on toggle and a hover/click ripple on top read as accidental motion. Both states now use `variant="text"` (no border ever), and a fixed `minWidth` keeps the button from reflowing as the label flips `Group control` ⇄ `Exit group`. `Edit` / `Cancel` got the same `whiteSpace: 'nowrap'` + `minWidth` treatment for symmetry. The `0 of 19 selected · select all - unselect all` line below the tag input now briefly pulses gold (0.7 s, fades to transparent) whenever the filtered selected count actually changes, so selecting a device gives a visible "it landed" cue. The chip/select-all row keeps its 28 px minHeight and never reflows. `UI_STYLE.md` §5 / §9.6 updated to forbid `text ⇄ outlined` toggles on header buttons. In plain words: the three mode-toggles are now indistinguishable, and the count line tells you when it changes · [BUG-0443](../bugs/BUG-0443-2026-10-09-edit-group-exit-coherence-and-count-flash.md)
- **Device Control header toggles become icons, and the Edit workflow moves to a reserved bottom slot** — the two adjacent text buttons in the page header (`Group control` and `Edit`) become two 40×40 icon buttons (dashboard, pencil) that never change width and never change label. The active mode gets a soft gold tint (`accent` at ~15 % alpha) — no border, no pulse, no hover ripple. Tooltips on hover carry the full meaning (`Take control of several devices together` / `Exit group` / `Edit tag` / `Exit Edit mode`). The Edit tag input, the chips/count row, and the `Save` button move from the top header to a new reserved bottom slot (56 px sticky bar); the `GroupSelectBar` (already at the bottom) now lives in the same slot. The slot is always 56 px tall — empty in idle, filled in either mode — so the device grid never shifts up or down when a mode is toggled. Edit and Group are now mutually exclusive (entering one exits the other). The filters and View menu now always show in the top header, so the user can filter while editing. `UI_STYLE.md` §5 gets a new "Mode-toggle bar" row. In plain words: the two toggles read as toggles, the workflow bars share one slot, the grid never jumps · [BUG-0444](../bugs/BUG-0444-2026-10-09-rec-icon-toggles-and-bottom-slot.md)
- **Device Control mode bar moves from the bottom of the page to a reserved line in the header** — the Edit bar / `GroupSelectBar` was sitting in a sticky slot at the bottom of the page (BUG-0444), which read as a footer that interrupts the device list and stayed put while the user scrolled. The reserved line is now in the header area, between the page header and the device grid (same 56 px `minHeight`, no `position: sticky`); empty in idle, filled in either mode. The Edit and Group mutual exclusion is unchanged. `UI_STYLE.md` §5 "Mode-toggle bar" row updated. In plain words: the mode bar lives with the header, not under the grid · [BUG-0445](../bugs/BUG-0445-2026-10-10-rec-bar-above-grid.md)
- **`system-ops` skill no longer duplicates masterchief ops (no second unaudited path to `service.restart`)** — the first commit of the system-ops skill shipped 4 MCP tools, two of which (`list_services`, `restart_service`) overlapped with the masterchief agent at the op level. Same operation, two surfaces, different security tiers: masterchief = confirm card + `masterchief_audit` row + prod `require_platform_admin`; system-ops = direct MCP call with no audit and no admin gate. That asymmetry was the exact `MCPAPIClient` service-key bypass TASK-71 was designed to close — `service.restart` had an unaudited second path through the MCP proxy. The 2 duplicates are now deleted from `system_ops_tools.py`; the skill's SKILL.md, triggers, and anti-patterns explicitly escalate every list / restart / status / log intent to `propose_masterchief_op`. system-ops is now strictly read-only-host-health (`check_storage` + `ping_host`); masterchief is the only surface for any audit-worthy op. Host routes are unchanged; the 8 remaining tests are green. In plain words: there's one way to restart a service, and it goes through the card · [BUG-0450](../bugs/BUG-0450-2026-10-09-system-ops-masterchief-op-overlap.md)
- **Dashboard and Device Control share their filters** — the Targets, Models and Tags picks (and the tag AND/OR mode) are stored once and shown on both pages; a target is now a device on both, and the Dashboard keeps a host when any of its devices is picked. The Dashboard "errors only" toggle and the Device Control page size stay per page. In plain words: filter once, both pages show the same devices
- **The VM summary block now shows live CPU / RAM / disk for the infra VMs** — `GET /server/system/vmMetrics` reads the latest row per VM from the `vm_metrics` table (windowed to the last hour, deduped on `vm`) and the Status page's top-of-page summary fills in numbers for frontend / proxy / database / storage the same way it already did for the server. Cells stay '—' until the collector has written at least one row for that VM, and the row's status dot picks up any 80% / 90% threshold breach from the metrics. The collector writes one row per VM every 5 minutes; no new probe, no new agent. In plain words: you can see if the storage box is hot without expanding its accordion
- **Status page opens with a one-glance VM summary** — a compact block above the existing accordions lists every machine the page already shows (server, frontend, proxy, database, storage, and every connected host) with its name, a status dot (ok / warn / down at the 80% / 90% thresholds) and CPU, 1-minute load, RAM and disk columns. Fixed-width cells and tabular-nums keep the block steady across the 15-second refresh, missing values show '—', and clicking a row opens and scrolls to its accordion below. The block follows the gold palette in light and dark and wraps to cards at 390 px. No new endpoint, no new fetch. In plain words: you can tell at a glance whether anything is hot without expanding five accordions
- **Atlas chat thumbs get an optional comment** — after a thumbs-up or thumbs-down, a small inline field opens under that message ("What was good?" / "What went wrong?") with Send and a dismiss (x); Enter sends, Esc dismisses, and once sent the field hides behind a brief "Thanks" while the thumb stays highlighted. The same row is updated in `agent_feedback` (no duplicate insert, scoped by `team_id` + `agent_id` + message ref) and the comment goes into the same `comment` column. In plain words: a thumb is still one tap, but if you want to say why it's now one field away and it is logged
- **Last light / dark leftovers fixed** — disabled fields and buttons, switches, the CI/CD job squares and the navigation-node handles use the palette instead of white or grey borders in dark mode; the PROD chip, AVQ, mobile-app, Status, verification, remote and report status colours, agent badges and the analytics export use palette tokens instead of Material Design hex (about 240 places); the Virtual Scripts code editor's gutter and background match the page. In plain words: the light / dark audit now finds nothing off-palette except overlays drawn on video on purpose.
- **Pages stop jumping when data refreshes** — UI rules for stable layout (fixed slots for live text, tabular numbers, fixed table columns), a `ui-check` step in the frontend lint, and the first fixes: Logs "Last refreshed", numbers in tables and tiles, Flaky and Slow tests columns. In plain words: filters and buttons no longer move when a refresh changes the text next to them

- **Jev verdicts explain themselves** — a result Jev decides now carries its reasons: the comment says the confidence, the next-likeliest classes and the evidence (error lines, non-default arguments, host, duration), and the AI queue and Test Reports comment dialogs show the verdict chip, a probability bar per class, the evidence and the decision id. No extra LLM call; older rows keep their text. In plain words: you can see why the AI judged a failure as a system issue without opening the database
- **`android_mobile` over `adb screencap` — no HDMI, no AVD, no scrcpy** — a new per-device systemd template `vpt-emulator-fifo@<device_id>.service` runs the `adb -s $serial exec-out screencap -p` loop (same script the emulator fleet uses) and writes each device's latest frame to its own path; `run_ffmpeg.sh`'s `imagefile` branch reads it and produces the ordinary HLS / captures / thumbnails. One service instance per slot, all driven from the host `.env` (`DEVICEn_ADB_SERIAL` + `DEVICEn_VIDEO`), so a Mac with two USB-tethered handsets becomes a real two-phone VPT host with no hardware grabber and no AVD. `emulator_screencap.sh` keeps its `emulator-5554` default for the existing emulator fleet, the installer's lookup now prefers a `@` template when one exists for the same name, and the new "Real Android handset over `adb screencap`" block in `backend_host/src/.env.example` ships the full recipe ([EMULATOR.md](../agent/devices/EMULATOR.md), [STREAM.md §3](../agent/devices/STREAM.md)). In plain words: a real phone streams into VPT through the same imagefile pipeline the Android emulator fleet already uses.

- **The mobile APK republishes itself** — a timer on codebox rebuilds the single download `vpt-mobile-app-debug.apk` within 5 minutes of any push to `main` that touches the app or the frontend, and keeps one signed APK per `release-*` tag under `downloads/releases/` (no backfill, never pruned). In plain words: the download link always has the latest app and nobody has to build it by hand ([README](../../features/mobile-app/app/README.md))
- **A bot plays Super Kirby Clash on the Nintendo Switch, from any screen, fight after fight** — `game/game_kirby` reads the HDMI frames (Kirby, the boss, both health bars), attacks, closes in and guards on its own, and `--start any --fights N` walks the menus by itself (village → quest board → Seaside → Hornhead → Set Off) and chains N fights through "Play the same quest". It never presses A in the Shoppe and leaves purchase prompts on Cancel. `--chooser jev` lets the Jev decision model pick the bot's tactic every few seconds (logged with outcomes). Scripts can now press Switch buttons through the action executor (`action_type: "switch"`), and every report step carries its screenshots: one every 3 s in a fight, one per screen in the menus. In plain words: it beat the first boss on its own several times, in 25–78 s ([GAME_BOTS](../agent/game/GAME_BOTS.md), [SWITCH_GAMES](../agent/game/SWITCH_GAMES.md), [TASK-51](../tasks/TASK-51-tetris-any-platform.md))
- **Every generated page follows the app's look and theme** — KPI, zap, restart-video, heatmap, subtitle-QA, game and drone reports, DOM / auto-build graph reports, the shared-report password and banner, the CI/CD run index, the API test report, the API docs pages and the security dashboard now use one shared palette (`report_theme.py`): warm grey page and white cards in light, the app's dark palette in dark, no navy or blue; they follow the app's light / dark setting and browsers no longer auto-darken them. Also in the app: toasts follow the theme (info toast gold, not blue), the embedded Grafana dashboard switches to dark with the app instead of staying white, the API docs page uses palette method colours, and the VNC viewer's slate-blue bar is neutral. In plain words: no page opened from VirtualPyTest is stuck in the old blue look or blinds you in dark mode. Host-built reports need a host deploy to pick it up.
- **Atlas writes virtual scripts** — ask Atlas to "create a script that …" or "edit script SC_…": the new `create-virtual-script` skill reads the app's real node names, writes the Python from the platform template, saves it with `save_virtual_script` (syntax errors come back with their line and Atlas fixes them) and runs it on a device when asked; `list_scripts` / `execute_script` now carry `virtual_script_id`, so Atlas can run any virtual script, not only disk ones. Ships with the `virtual-scripts` feature; server deploy. In plain words: Atlas can now write a test script for you and run it, with no deploy
- **Test and campaign reports use the app's look** — warm grey page with white cards in light, the app's dark palette in dark, a neutral header with a hairline instead of the blue banner, gold links and selection, palette pass / fail / skipped colours, and no more cyan, pink or purple step markers. Reports are built on the host: new reports get the look after a host deploy, old ones keep theirs. In plain words: a report now looks like the rest of VirtualPyTest.
- **Light mode: more contrast on tabs, alerts, rails and Quick Test** — page tab bars (Logs, Analytics, Settings, Run summary, Teams, Users, Workspaces, Device info, AVQ) sit on a card strip with the gold underline; info alerts are neutral cards with a gold icon instead of a blue box (navy in dark); side rails and device tiles are white cards; Quick Test shows the test as a header card (title, variables on a soft inset) and a steps card. In plain words: in light mode nothing important floats on the grey page any more.
- **Navigation editor nodes follow the theme** — screen, menu, dialog, popup and overlay nodes are dark cards in dark mode and white cards in light mode, each with a strip on top in its type's colour (the root keeps a red strip and title); the current position is outlined in gold instead of purple. In plain words: the navigation map no longer shows a wall of light-blue boxes in dark mode.
- **Every page reviewed in light and dark** — the navy, slate and blue leftovers are gone from the navigation editor panels, AI agent chat and its flow viewers, device control and stream header, builders' side panels and dialogs, verification dialogs, reports, monitoring, settings, admin pages and the AVQ, companion, AI dataset and mobile-app pages: surfaces, hairlines, selection, running state and links come from the palette; blue count chips and icon buttons become neutral, gold text on white uses the readable gold, the heatmap's current hour and the reports' "running" status are gold, and the security report follows the theme. In plain words: light and dark now look like the same product on every page; only the navigation editor's pastel node colours are left for a decision.
- **Fire TV support: Fire OS and Vega OS sticks** — a Fire TV model is now named by product + OS: classic **Fire OS** sticks (Android) are `fire_tv_fireos` (was `fire_tv`; same adb remote, same `android_tv` family); Amazon's new **Vega OS** sticks are not Android, so the Android remote failed on them and they get `fire_tv_vega` with its own `vega` remote. It reaches the stick with the host's ordinary adb (USB or network, no Vega SDK and no Amazon login on the host) and sends keys, text, app launch / stop, the app list and a device screenshot with Vega's own tools (`inputd-cli`, `vmsgr`, `vlcm`); Take Control shows the Fire TV remote. Set `DEVICEn_MODEL=fire_tv_vega` (or `fire_tv_fireos`, replacing `fire_tv`) with `DEVICEn_ADB_SERIAL` (or `DEVICEn_IP`); developer mode is turned on once on the TV. Keep a stick awake: Fire OS `adb shell settings put secure sleep_timeout 0`; Vega has no shell setting, so set Display & Sounds → Ambient Experience → Advanced → Timeout = Never ([FIRE_TV_VEGA.md](../agent/devices/FIRE_TV_VEGA.md), [TASK-57](../tasks/TASK-57-vega-firetv.md)). In plain words: both kinds of Fire TV stick can be tested like any other TV box · 🗄 DB migration `20261005c_vega_tv_device_model.sql` + `20261005d_fire_tv_models_by_os.sql`
- **Hide the footer's “Get the mobile app” and “View on GitHub” links** — two switches in Settings → Branding (saved on the server, applies at once, no rebuild); `VITE_SHOW_MOBILE_APP_LINK=false` / `VITE_SHOW_GITHUB_LINK=false` set the default. In plain words: white-label deployments can drop both links from the footer.
- **Fields, chips and tables follow the palette in both themes** — outlined inputs and chips get the palette hairline instead of MUI's white (dark) and grey (light) borders, table cell lines and progress-bar tracks match, and MUI's pink "secondary" colour (TC/TP tags, Latest, Admin role chips) becomes a muted purple from the palette. In plain words: no more white outlines around fields in dark mode and no more hot-pink tags; first step of the full light/dark page review (TASK-56).
- **Light mode reviewed: white top bar, more contrast** — the blue top bar is gone (white bar with a hairline, gold active item, like dark mode's neutral bar); the page is a warm grey so white cards and tables stand out, hairlines, table headers and grey text are darker. Table headers now follow the theme in dark mode too. In plain words: light mode is as calm and readable as dark mode, with no stray blue.
- **BLE remote pairing: a green light when the remote is ready** — after Start Pairing the panel shows "Preparing the remote…" while the host resets the adapter (~30 s), then a green "Ready to pair" with a 2-minute countdown for the STB; a setup error (e.g. advertising refused) stops the wait and is shown. The logs window's third tab now shows the last start.sh / resume.sh run instead of the boot-only service, which was always empty. In plain words: click first, wait for green, then put the STB in pairing mode
- **Test and Campaign builders use the gold and grey look** — the START block, block surfaces, selection, running highlight, zoom controls, mini-map, sidebar, header and progress overlay drop the old navy and blue for the app palette, in dark and light. In plain words: the builder canvas now looks like the rest of the app.
- **BLE remote address per device (`DEVICEn_BLE_MAC`)** — each BLE remote can be given its own address in the host `.env`, used on the next Start Pairing; without it a new adapter now starts at `1F:11` / `1F:12` (by adapter number) instead of `1F:01` on every robot, and adapters already paired keep their address. In plain words: robots next to each other no longer present the same remote, so an STB stops wandering to the neighbouring robot — as long as each robot gets its own addresses
- **Test menu: Quality and Run Management pages with tabs, and a cleaner click in the top bar** — Flaky / Slow / Not executed are one Quality page (`/test-results/quality?tab=…`), and Groups / Milestones / Labels / Defects / Custom reports / Manual runs are one Run Management page (`/test-results/run-management?tab=…`); each Run Management tab opens with one sentence saying what it is for and an empty tab offers the button to create the first item; the old URLs redirect to their tab (query kept), and the Test menu's Report column is now Test Reports, Quality, Run Management, Model Reports, Dependency Report, CI/CD Reports. Top-bar buttons, the Server / Workspace selects, dropdown and mega-menu items and the user menu no longer show the cropped MUI ripple: a flat hover / pressed background instead. In plain words: nine report entries became two pages with tabs that explain themselves, and clicking the menu no longer flashes a clipped circle
- **Reports: one icon set everywhere, and a header that no longer jumps on a language switch** — the script, testcase and campaign report theme switch is now sun / moon / monitor icons (the word is the tooltip), so switching EN/FR/DE no longer changes its width; emoji in the execution summary and report labels (✅ ❌ ⚠️ 🎯 🌐 📺 ⏱ 📊 📋 📸 🔗 ℹ️ …) are drawn as the same line icons as the links block, pass green, fail red, warning amber, the rest in the main text colour; the links block icons and labels use the main text colour instead of grey. Scripts, logs and translation keys are unchanged. New runs only (host deploy). In plain words: reports look cleaner and the header stays still when you change language
- **Localize: a low-confidence answer is kept only when Jev confirms it; otherwise unknown** — a single answer under 0.80 confidence (the overlay and the Localize button, same verdict) now needs Jev to pick the same screen at 0.9 or more; on 375 stb_tv frames the 8 wrong answers disappear (replay/tvshop read as home, profile as settings) and 21 of 24 right ones stay. With Jev off those answers read "unknown". Verdicts are cached 120 s per screen and capped at 120 calls a minute.
- **Freeze alerts: Jev triage in shadow, from facts the host gathers on the alert** — with `ALERT_JEV_FACTS_ENABLED=true` on a host, each new freeze alert gets `jev_facts` (how much the picture changes between its frames, and the words OCR reads, except on cameras); the server's alert triage (`vpt-discard-incidents`) shows them to Jev and logs whether it would keep or dismiss the alert (`ALERT_JEV_MODE`, default `shadow`), changing nothing. Offline, on 106 labelled strips: real freezes kept 2/2, false alerts dismissed 87%. Off with `JEV_ENABLED=false` or `JEV_DISABLED_USAGES=text.decision.freeze_triage`. Restart `vpt-monitor` (hosts) and `vpt-discard-incidents` (server) ([TASK-55](../tasks/TASK-55-jev-freeze-alert-triage.md))
- **AI Dataset: real freeze examples on demand** — `features/ai-dataset/scripts/freeze_injection.py` plays a test HLS stream full-screen on a VNC host, freezes the player and records the frames as labelled freeze and moving strips. The dataset had no real freeze before: every monitor alert had been a false alarm (TASK-27)
- **Jev second opinion, logged only, on localize ties and borderline text checks** — when localize cannot pick one screen (a tie, low confidence, or none) or a text check scores within 0.10 of its threshold, Jev is asked in the background and its answer is logged next to the rule's for the agreement report; nothing changes, nothing waits (bounded, rate-capped, off with `JEV_ENABLED=false` or `JEV_DISABLED_USAGES`). In plain words: we collect evidence on whether Jev would fix these shaky cases before it is allowed to
- **One switch to turn Jev off: `JEV_ENABLED=false` in the server `.env`** — Jev is on unless switched off; off, no Jev call is made anywhere (server or hosts), nothing is logged or billed, and Sherlock, test-prompt auto, the companion meter and the game runs keep their normal path (Sherlock simply uses its LLM). `JEV_DISABLED_USAGES` turns off single uses; `GET /server/ai/config` shows `jev_enabled` (TASK-47 G12). In plain words: sites without AI, or that do not want Jev, can switch it off in one place
- **Companion fact check meter** — with `DEVICE{N}_COMPANION_FACTCHECK=true`, every subtitled sentence of a live programme is scored by Jev (factual claim, evasive, contradicts self, emotional appeal, dodged question; ~$0.00004 and ~0.5 s per sentence) and given a speaker from the people the channel's captions named for that programme; only sentences Jev rates as factual claims go to the sourced web check. The news and debate cards show it as a panel per speaker beside the live sentence, then the checked claims with their verdict and source. Every decision is logged (`ai_decisions`) for a later evaluation; scores are not calibrated yet. The companion strip and its cards now use the app palette (gold accent, theme greys; new `series` token for per-speaker colours) (TASK-45). In plain words: a live, cheap fact check panel under the TV picture
- **Companion: Jev decides before three paid calls** — a decision model (~$0.00004 per question) now gates the companion's web fact check (skips vague claims and claims already checked this programme), its programme-facts web lookup (skips headline straps, channel names and generic slots such as "Weather") and its 2-minute digest (skips it when nothing new was said). A gate can only skip; if Jev fails or is unsure the call is made as before. Every decision is logged (`text.decision.companion.gate`). Also: the meter reads the live transcript on talk shows without subtitles, never reads yesterday's transcript file, and only the channel info bar can mark a channel change (TASK-45). In plain words: the companion spends less on calls that would not have told you anything
- **Top bar: the current page is a gold tab underline** — the grey box behind the active item is gone; its label turns gold (dark mode) and a gold bar sits on the bottom edge of the top bar, with a faint white bar on hover. Every item now spans the bar's full height, so the menus still open right under it. In plain words: a cleaner marker for the page you're on
- **One UI style for the whole app, dialogs and popups included** — the MUI theme now uses the gold palette as `primary` in light and dark, so every dialog, menu, select list, tab, switch, checkbox, link and primary button follows it without page code (they were MUI blue). Dialogs get a hairline frame, a 1.1rem title and spaced actions (the old white button borders, invisible in light mode, are gone); menus tint the selected item gold; tooltips are one dark chip in both themes; cards have a hairline instead of a drop shadow. The top bar keeps its own colours. The rules are written down in [UI_STYLE.md](../agent/platform/UI_STYLE.md) (palette tokens, type sizes, which component for titles, tiles, switches, status, pills, dialogs, icons, and a ship checklist), pointed to from AGENTS.md, MAP and PATTERNS, and made binding as contract #28. In plain words: popups now match the pages, and every agent has one guide to build new screens the same way
- **Configuration and admin pages aligned: Logs, Device Models, Settings, Code Deployment, Run Command, System Status, Mobile app, Tenants, Teams, Users, Roles, Workspaces** — same title size, hairline cards and theme-following tables as the other pages; header icons are gold, primary buttons (Add model, Create team, Save…) are gold instead of blue, tabs underline in gold, switches and checkboxes are gold when on. `pageSurfaceSx` (PageChrome) now carries those control colours so a page gets them by wrapping itself once. In plain words: the settings and admin side looks like the rest of the app
- **Dashboard, Device, AI Dataset and the builders aligned with the app palette** — Dashboard: the host and server cards no longer draw hard-coded light-grey borders (they glared in dark mode), host icons are gold and cards have a hairline border. Device: title with a count pill and a gold Edit. AI Dataset (training review): gold progress pill and screen name, the "No, fix it" button is outlined so "Yes, correct" stands out, palette accordions. Test Builder and Campaign Builder: the Visual / AI switch and the builder name are gold, and Run is gold by default everywhere (it was blue on these builders). The AI Agent page already used this palette and is unchanged. In plain words: one look across the app, and dark mode without bright borders
- **"Create a Grafana dashboard" builds it for you** — asked to create/make/build a dashboard, Atlas loads its `grafana-dashboard` skill, picks catalog sources for each panel (never SQL), builds ONE dashboard from the house template `infra/monitoring/grafana/dashboards/template.json` and pushes it to the temporary "Atlas drafts" folder; the answer carries the link, the expiry and which filters are dashboard variables. The template defines the variables (Workspace, Host, Device, User Interface, Environment, Tag), time range, styling and grid; every filter dropdown works on every panel that has the column (All never drops rows), and a filter you named, such as a device, is preselected so you can change it in Grafana. A customer overlay that ships its own `template.json` at the same path replaces it at deploy; the template is never pushed as a dashboard. No new env keys, no migration. In plain words: say "create a dashboard of pass rate by device and incidents over time" and get a Grafana link ([ATLAS_ANALYTICS](../agent/ai/ATLAS_ANALYTICS.md), [TASK-43](../tasks/TASK-43-atlas-semantic-analytics.md))
- **Atlas canvases come with a Grafana draft** — when Atlas draws a canvas (two or more panels), the server also builds the matching Grafana dashboard from the same trusted catalog queries, pushes it into a temporary "Atlas drafts" folder (view-only for non-admins) and the canvas header shows "Open in Grafana" with "draft · expires <date>" (24 h). If every panel already exists in a maintained dashboard, that dashboard is linked instead and nothing is pushed. The Pi-hosted server forwards the push to the main server, which holds the Grafana credentials; a Grafana problem never blocks the chart (the reply says "Grafana draft not created: …"). Keeping a draft stays manual. The Report export includes the link. No new env keys (uses `GRAFANA_URL` / `GRAFANA_ADMIN_*` / `GRAFANA_DOMAIN` on the main server). In plain words: ask for a canvas, get a Grafana dashboard you can open, tweak and keep ([ATLAS_ANALYTICS](../agent/ai/ATLAS_ANALYTICS.md), [TASK-43](../tasks/))
- **Seven more pages restyled: Model Reports, Dependency Report, Alerts, Heatmap, AI Queue, Analytics, Interface** — same look as the Test pages: a plain title with a one-line description, numbers as stat tiles instead of a "label value" text row, Tests/Views switches as gold segmented controls, hairline cards, and tables whose header follows the theme in dark mode. Alerts shows incident types as tinted pills (red freeze, amber audio loss; closed ones muted with a green tick) and section counts as pills; Heatmap's Audio/Video Yes/No are tinted pills and All/OK/KO is a switch; Dependency Report's tiles switch the view and rates are coloured text instead of chips; AI Queue shows analysed / discarded / kept / pending as tiles. The shared pieces live in `frontend/src/components/common/PageChrome.tsx` (PageTitle, StatTiles, pageSurfaceSx, segmentedSx, goldButtonSx). In plain words: the monitoring and results pages now look like one app
- **Test Reports restyled** — a plain title with a Tests / Campaigns / Shared links switch (gold when selected), Quick Stats as two tiles (success rate coloured by threshold, average duration) with a "Grafana dashboard" link, hairline cards, a table header that follows the theme in dark mode, palette hover and status colours, and Report / Logs with their own icons (they were two identical blue and pink link icons). In plain words: easier to tell the report link from the logs link
- **Shared report snapshots keep the link list aligned** — in a shared copy the "Execution Logs / Metadata / Verification review / Code source" rows, whose links are not included, used to put the "(not included in the snapshot)" note in a grid cell of its own and push every following label and value out of place; the note now sits inside the link's own cell. Applies to snapshots created after the next server deploy; existing shared copies are frozen and keep the old layout
- **Test report: Save as PDF and Share from the report itself** — two round icon buttons at the top right of every run report, after Light/Dark/System: a down arrow that opens the browser's print dialog (the page prints light, sections open, controls hidden, so "Save as PDF" gives the whole report) and a share icon that opens the Share dialog of the web app for that run (`/test-results/reports?share=<script result id>`), so an old run no longer has to be found in the Test Reports list first. The share icon needs a login and the share permission, and is dropped from shared snapshot copies; the campaign report gets the PDF arrow only. Reports generated before this change do not have the buttons
- **Build Campaign restyled** — a plain title with a gold "Editing <name>" pill while a campaign is loaded, numbered form steps (1 Details, 2 Add tests), a gold Save/Update, and an empty run order that says to pick tests above (it used to point at a + button this page hides). Existing campaigns show their count; the loaded one is highlighted in gold and every row has a gold Load. In plain words: clearer what to fill in first and which campaign you are editing
- **Run Tests and Monitor Tests restyled** — both pages open on a plain title with a Tests / Campaigns switch (gold when selected) instead of blue chips; cards have a hairline border instead of a shadow. Run is the gold button on Run Tests and Abort is red. Last Executions (on both pages) shows the status as a coloured word (Passed, Failed, Running, Queued, Aborted) instead of a bare icon, Report and Logs have their own icons instead of two identical link icons, rows tint on hover, and the table header follows the theme in dark mode. Monitor Tests shows each section's count as a pill (gold while tests are running). In plain words: easier to see what passed and to open the right link
- **Coverage page redesign** — the numbers are cards (Requirements, Covered with its %, Tests linked, Gaps, Avg pass rate) above a gold overall bar; clicking Gaps, or the All / Gaps / Covered switch, lists only the requirements without a test (or with one). Categories sit in one list sorted lowest coverage first (or by name), each with a coloured bar, covered/total, test count, a red "N gaps" badge and the pass rate once opened; a category's numbers always count all its requirements, whatever the filter. Requirement rows show a status dot and an outlined priority tag. In plain words: the categories that need tests come first, and one click shows only what is missing
- **Quick Test builder redesign** — the saved-tests rail has a search box and a + for a new test, shows interface · variant under each name, and shows delete only on hover. The test name is the page title with a summary line (interface · variant · steps · loops · variables). The AI edit is one bar with a sparkle and a gold Edit. Steps form a numbered timeline: each step type has an icon, the row actions (run step, move, delete) light up on hover, and while a run is in progress the step being run is outlined in gold, passed in green, failed in red. A loop reads "Repeat N times" in a gold-tinted group, and one dashed Add strip holds every step type plus Loop. Run is gold (the shared builder header takes an optional Run style; Test Builder and Campaigns keep theirs). In plain words: the same builder, easier to follow step by step
- **CI/CD Reports opens on the last 24 hours** — the page reads only that range from the server (68 runs instead of 200 today, about a third of the data), and widening the range loads the rest on demand
- **Virtual Scripts editor redesign** — the script list has a search box (scripts or folders), folders show their count, each script carries a dot for where it lives (prod gold, test, dev) with a legend, and + creates a script. The header shows the lifecycle as `dev › test › prod vN` (prod opens its rollback history) next to a gold Promote menu; Delete moved into the ⋯ menu, Save turns gold when there are unsaved changes and Run is the page's gold button. The editor opens on `folder / name` as its title with a Syntax OK / Error line N label, the description as one editable line and the script's parameters under it; Script / Doc is a segmented control. After a Run, a bar under the editor keeps how it ended (passed / failed / error), on which device, how long, with Report and Logs. In plain words: easier to scan, and the last run's result stays on screen
- **Test Prompt page redesign** — the sidebar groups prompts by interface, has a search box, shows each prompt's version, mode and last edit, and shows delete only on hover. The header merges the version chips into one (`v1 · dev`) and Promote becomes a quiet gold link. The name is the page title, the target screen reads "Starts on screen …", and Prompt / Pass when are two inset cards. The run modes are a segmented control and Run stays the one gold button. Results show a pass rate and a strip of the last 20 runs; each run is a row in one list (status in colour, no more red or green card borders), Report and Logs are icons, and a failed run shows the first line of its error under the row. Colours come from the AI-pages palette in light and dark mode (`accentText` and `warning` were added for readable gold text on white and for run errors). In plain words: the same page, easier to read, and you see why a run failed without opening the report
- **The web app in English, French and German** — at the bottom of the Settings menu (and the phone's More menu), a Theme row (light / dark / system icons) and a Language row (EN / FR / DE) switch the app at once, without a reload. The choice is kept in the browser under the same key as virtualpytest.com and the test reports, so reports opened from the app open in the same language (`#lang=` on the link). First visit follows the browser language. This first step covers the top bar, its menus, the phone bottom bar and the Dashboard; the other pages follow page by page, and a CI check (`frontend/scripts/i18n-check.mjs`) fails on a missing translation or a new English literal in a translated page. In plain words: pick Français or Deutsch in Settings and the app's menus and Dashboard are in that language ([user guide](../user-guide/app-languages.md), [APP_I18N](../agent/platform/APP_I18N.md), [TASK-53](../tasks/TASK-53-app-i18n.md))
- **Atlas routing log kept 30 days** — the stored routing picks (with the first 500 chars of each prompt) are pruned per team, hourly, after 30 days · `1ec9758a7`
- **Navigation editor: "AI Generate" is now "Build Map"** — the button maps the device's current screen with fixed rules (UI dump on Android/web, no AI model; a vision model only on TV/STB); it is not the AI agent pipeline (Atlas `build-tree-*`, QualiAI). The MCP tool `auto_discover_screen` says the same, and the MCP map-builder doc was rewritten around the one tool that exists ([map builder vs agent](../mcp/mcp_tools_exploration.md#map-builder-vs-agent-pipeline)) (TASK-40)
- **Test reports in English, French and German** — the run report and the campaign report carry an `EN | FR | DE` switcher at the top right (next to Light/Dark/System). It remembers the choice (`virtualpytest:language`, shared with the web app), accepts `#lang=fr` in the link (the fragment, so signed storage URLs stay valid), and otherwise follows the browser language. The platform's words live in `shared/src/lib/i18n/report/<code>.json` (one file per language; a new file adds a button). A script translates its own step names and summary lines with a `<script>.i18n.json` next to it (`goto` and `subtitle_qa` ship one), or with `main._i18n = {...}` in its source, which is how a virtual script does it. Testcase reports get the switcher too: block, navigation and loop lines are translated. Device, node and file names, OCR text and numbers stay as captured. The translations are inside the page, so shared snapshot links keep the switcher. `scripts/report_i18n_check.py` lists missing keys. In plain words: open any new report and click FR or DE ([TASK-52](../tasks/TASK-52-report-i18n.md))
- **Drone showcase: two Tellos play hide-and-seek and race to find an object** — device model `tello` (AV `rtsp_stream` on `udp://0.0.0.0:<port>`, remote `tello` with every move through a safety guard: ceiling, geofence, room-map furniture, drone separation, battery/temperature gates, keepalive, land on any error), a flight panel in Remote, and two scripts, `drone/drone_hide_and_seek` and `drone/drone_object_race`, where each drone has its own agent and the platform referee confirms the win on a fresh frame. Everything runs without a drone against a wire-compatible Tello simulator (`test_scripts/drone/local/run_local.py`, HTML report); `scripts/drone/tello_netns.sh` puts two Tellos (both 192.168.10.1) on one host. `run_ffmpeg.sh` now treats `udp://` sources as live feeds, which takes effect after a `vpt-stream` restart. In plain words: the drones are not here yet, but on the day they arrive the demos only need the hardware ([TASK-32](../tasks/TASK-32-drone-device-showcase.md), [runbook](../tasks/TASK-32-arrival-day-runbook.md))
- **Per-workspace heatmaps** — with a workspace selected, the Heatmap page shows only that workspace's devices. `vpt-heatmap` reads the workspaces of its server every minute and, besides the global mosaic, composes one mosaic per workspace that owns a current device, stored as `heatmaps/<server>/ws/<workspace_id>/<HHMM>.jpg` and `.json` (no `_ok`/`_ko` per workspace, to spare storage inodes, so the OK/KO filter is hidden there). Every frame is now downloaded once per cycle and shared by ALL, OK, KO and every workspace mosaic, and a workspace failure never marks the global cycle failed. Reports generated from a workspace frame keep the `ws/<id>` path (the id is validated as a UUID). A new workspace shows the usual no-data state for earlier minutes. In plain words: pick a workspace and the heatmap shows just its devices; "All" still shows everything
- **Subtitle QA as a test script** — `subtitle_qa.py` runs on a *local video* device: the device plays a film with its subtitle track drawn in, from second 0, and the script checks about 630 frames over 10 minutes (dense around each subtitle start and end) for missing / unexpected / stuck / wrong / truncated / late / early / wrong-language / overlapping / low-contrast subtitles, with tesseract OCR and the film's speech timing; no AI cost. It copies the frames while the film plays and analyses them afterwards on the host (80–95 s on vpt-pi4), then re-reads every flagged frame from the source file to tell a device problem from an OCR limit. The run report links a **Subtitle Report**: verdict, issues grouped by cue with the subtitle band enlarged, an error mosaic (also the run's Test Mosaic), what was not counted and why, how the subtitles were read (OCR version, resolutions) and a glossary. `scripts/subtitle_qa/run.py` does the same on a local .mkv without a device. `run_ffmpeg.sh` draws a soft subtitle track when `<file>.subtrack` exists and restarts a device's file on a new restart token in `active_video_path.conf`; hosts need a `vpt-stream` restart to pick it up ([user guide](../user-guide/subtitle-qa.md), [agent doc](../agent/execution/SUBTITLE_QA.md), [TASK-50](../tasks/TASK-50-subtitle-qa.md))
- **Run report summary in one font with one icon set** — the Execution Summary text, its links (logs, metadata, review, code, reports, mosaic) and the code version / trigger now share the report font and one left edge, each link with a small line icon that follows light/dark mode instead of mixed emoji; section titles (Test Mosaic, Zap summary, Edge results, Error details) use the same icons. Language files keep plain labels
- **Device Control scales past 16 devices** — the grid is paginated (12 / 16 / 24 / 48 per page, remembered). A page with more than 16 tiles shows each device's latest frame, refreshed once a second, instead of a live stream (48 tiles: ~0.5 browser cores and 0.33 Mbps instead of 1.4 cores, 4.2 Mbps and over half the players never starting); hold the mouse on a tile 400 ms to watch it live. Off-screen tiles neither poll nor stream. Every capture pipeline now writes `thumbnails/latest.jpg` (`run_ffmpeg.sh`), which takes effect only after a `vpt-stream` restart: a host deploy does not restart it, run `update_core.sh --restart vpt-stream` after it ([TASK-48](../tasks/TASK-48-device-control-scale.md)) · `dbb407f22` `a3116a55f` `bfe165533` `2cbbee136`
- **AI Dataset: Jev reviews screens before Sonnet** — `triage_batch.py import-jev` settles open screen samples from a `scripts/jev_review` run (Jev picks the screen from reference-match, OCR and seed-frame facts; 94% on the human-checked rows against 39% for the cloud vision model, about $0.0001 per decision). Only answers at confidence ≥ 0.8 settle a row, as training labels, never as an independent check; the rest goes to the Sonnet second pass as before. The stb4 acceptance pipeline's temporary tree copies are collected and filed under `stb_tv` (`fold_temp_trees.py`), since they are the same STB screens (TASK-27)
- **Share one test report as an expiring link** — a Share button on every report (Test Reports rows and each script row of an expanded campaign) gives a link (`/server/share/<key>/`) that shows that report (steps, screenshots, verification images, video) to anyone with it, no VPT account needed, for 1 h / 1 d / 7 d / 30 d, with an optional password (8+ characters). The link is a frozen copy under `shared-snapshots/` in the bucket, so it outlives the 14-day report cleanup. A new **Shared links** tab on Test Reports lists every link of the team (active, expired, revoked) with views, size and quota use, and revokes one in a click; revoking deletes the copy at once. Logs, script source and links back into VPT are never included, typed values (text, PIN, passwords, URL tokens) are masked, only the report's own files are copied, and the page is sandboxed and `noindex`. Off unless the server has `REPORT_SHARING_ENABLED=true`; creating a link needs the new `reports.tests:share` permission (tester and above). A proxy that adds `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` needs two `cross-origin` locations, see the agent doc ([user guide](../user-guide/sharing-reports.md), [agent doc](../agent/platform/REPORT_SHARING.md), [TASK-49](../tasks/TASK-49-report-share-snapshot.md)) · `4feedcf2a` · 🗄 DB migration `20261004b_report_snapshots.sql`
- **Every server has an "All" workspace** — always first in the workspace switcher (which is now always shown) and on the Workspaces page: every device, script and page your role allows. Built in, so it cannot be edited or deleted and never goes stale when devices are added
- **AI Generate gives the start node its screenshot too** — the screens it creates already saved theirs; `home` (which it does not create) now gets the screenshot taken at Start when it has none, so every node of the built tree shows one (TASK-40)
- **Network-source streams use the GPU on VAAPI hosts** — HLS / RTSP / video-file devices now decode the source and encode the HLS stream with VAAPI when the host has an H.264 encode entrypoint (`run_ffmpeg.sh`), as USB capture already did; a 720p60 source at HD went from 59% to 24% of a core on vpt-nipogi. Software libx264 stays the fallback (Pi 5)
- **Nintendo Switch driven as a Bluetooth Pro Controller** — a `nintendo_switch` device now takes input from a stock Switch (no custom firmware): the host emulates a Pro Controller with nxbt in the opt-in root daemon `vpt-procon` (`setup/local/linux/backend_host/install_procon.sh`, `--lan` to serve a vpt-host on another machine through `DEVICE{n}_SWITCH_PROCON_URL` / `_TOKEN`). The remote panel is a Pro Controller photo with clickable buttons, both sticks (4 directions + click), ZL/ZR above the bumpers, a connection status line and a Pair button; its header reads "Nintendo Switch", and the stream panels read "RTSP / HLS Stream" / "Local Video". The USB sys-botbase controller (`usb_botbase`, needed custom firmware, never usable) is removed ([NINTENDO_SWITCH](../agent/devices/NINTENDO_SWITCH.md), [TASK-31](../tasks/TASK-31-handover-nintendo-switch.md))
- **Sherlock keeps Jev's verdict when its LLM call fails** — a result whose LLM analysis errors (e.g. provider out of credits) gets Jev's verdict when Jev is at or above the 0.9 gate, instead of staying `ai_error` · `342206880`
- **Atlas records every routing pick** — each router-mode turn stores the skill chosen (or a direct answer), model and outcome in `ai_decisions` (usage `agent.atlas.route`), so routing can be measured · `ff5f961b2`
- **Sherlock sees the run's arguments, and a wrong argument reads as a script problem** — the analyzer and Jev now get each argument the run got, with its default and meaning from the script (`sherlock_args.py`); Jev's SCRIPT_ISSUE also covers *an argument whose value does not fit its meaning*. On 217 labelled results the hourly `dns_lookuptime` runs given a hostname as `--dns` went from 0/133 to 133/133 right, with no change on the other 84 ([JEV](../agent/ai/JEV.md))
- **AI Dataset verdicts and eval runs go to Langfuse** — when a human labels a sample that came from a real AI call (`banner`, `content-type`), its Langfuse trace gets a `verdict_human` score (did the model answer right?); a `triage_batch.py import` posts `verdict_ai` the same way for the rows it decides. `score_eval.py` pushes each run as a Langfuse dataset run (`vpt-eval-<family>`, one item per sample, scores `correct` / `focus_correct`), so model comparison over time is Langfuse's dataset view. Off with `--no-langfuse` or when tracing is off; a Langfuse error never fails a label, an import or a run (TASK-27)
- **Tree writes reach the hosts on demand: `POST /server/navigationTrees/<tree_id>/refresh`** — hosts read trees from a materialized view that the database refreshes once a minute, so a tree written through REST or MCP needed a ~90 s wait before navigation saw it. The new route refreshes the view now, reads the tree back, clears the server caches and the tree on every host, and answers with the node/edge counts and each host's result. In plain words: after editing a tree, call refresh instead of waiting · 🗄 DB migration `20261003a_refresh_navigation_trees_mv.sql`
- **The AI dataset no longer collects the game showcases** — 2048, Tetris, Flappy Bird and Pokémon frames are dropped at capture (`training_samples.EXCLUDED_USERINTERFACE_PATTERNS`), like phone frames: they are not TV or app screens and only cost review time (TASK-27)
- **Sherlock asks the Jev decision model first, and skips the LLM when Jev is sure** — the script-result analyzer now asks Jev (`text.decision.sherlock`) for the verdict before its LLM; at confidence 0.9 or more Jev's verdict is saved (`check_type` `ai_auto_jev`, comment names the decision) and the LLM isn't called; below it the LLM decides as before. Measured on 217 past results labelled by blind AI judges: Jev ≥0.9 was right 59/60, as often as the LLM; that covers about a third of analyses. 10% of Jev-decided results still run the LLM, logged only, to keep measuring. `ANALYZER_JEV_GATE` (default 0.9; above 1 turns it off) and `ANALYZER_JEV_AUDIT_PERCENT` (default 10) on the server; the change takes effect when `vpt-discard-scripts` restarts ([JEV](../agent/ai/JEV.md))
- **AI Dataset review: one Yes, one Fix, and the next sample at once** — a screen answer has a single Yes (saves the screen and its focused item together) and a single *No, fix it* (screen and focus in one form); the focus line no longer has its own Yes / Fix / `f`. An answer is saved in the background, so the next sample shows immediately instead of after the save (about 1 s through RPI1-server); a failed save brings that sample back with the error. The next two images are preloaded, and a sample's question waits for its image, so text and picture appear together. The freeze-strip answer buttons read as one line of text instead of two ragged columns (TASK-27)
- **Mobile layout: server picker on Dashboard and Device only; Settings renamed** — other mobile pages no longer start with the server dropdown; the page title is *Settings* (was *System Settings*) and its *Host & Devices* tab is *Host* (APK 1.0.49)
- **Mobile app: update banner in Settings** — inside the APK, Settings shows the installed version and, when a newer APK is published, an *Update available* banner whose **Update** downloads it for Android's installer. `npm run publish` (features/mobile-app/app) uploads an APK with its version file ([MOBILE_APP](../technical/MOBILE_APP.md#in-app-update-check))
- **Atlas analytics: dashboard presets for every monitoring area** — Atlas can now show, on request, 33 dashboard-equivalent charts and tables without SQL: failing testcases (also "for this device"), pass rate by device/script and its trend, duration trend, campaign and deployment outcomes and timeline, active alerts, incidents by type and over time, degraded hosts and fleet verdicts (latest check, not history), device occupancy and owners, latest device information, Ookla/DNS/Superping results by host, gateway, domain or target, host CPU/resources, and — for administrators — platform component status and availability. Charts can be bucketed per hour/day/week/month, combined into a canvas of up to 6 panels, and compared (baseline vs candidate, delta, % delta, samples, missing side) (TASK-43, [Atlas analytics](../agent/ai/ATLAS_ANALYTICS.md))
- **Atlas analytics: export a chart as CSV, JSON, SVG, PNG or a static report** — every Agent Chat chart/canvas has an export bar; CSV neutralises spreadsheet formulas, the report is a script-free HTML page with the chart, its data, where it came from and any readability fallback. Atlas can also return CSV/JSON itself (`export_analytics_data`) (TASK-43)
- **Atlas analytics: Grafana draft on request** — asked for a Grafana version of a chart, Atlas first points to the maintained dashboard panel that already shows it; otherwise it writes a temporary dashboard file under `tmp/atlas-grafana/<id>/` (validated: VirtualPyTest datasource only, catalog queries only, core panels only, no credentials; expires after 24 h). Atlas never publishes or provisions it — copy it into the dashboards folder yourself to keep it (TASK-43)
- **Test results: run summary, run groups, milestones and labels** — every script run, campaign execution and run group gets a summary page (`/test-results/runs/<kind>/<id>`: one verdict, counts over all children, 30-day trend, Tests / Statistics / Defects tabs). Completed runs can be organised into run groups (latest attempt per script + interface + environment + host/device, attempts counted separately, rerun all or failures only, safe to retry), milestones and team labels (1–20 characters, at most 10 per run, assigned by id). Groups and milestones are archived and restored, never deleted. Test Reports gains summary links, label / milestone / group filters, bulk assignment and flaky badges; rerun, checked, discard, report and log work as before. A campaign now records each script's outcome, including skipped scripts, so its summary counts them instead of guessing. Four new permissions (`reports.runs:edit`, `reports.defects:edit`, `reports.share:create`, `reports.manual:execute`) are granted to manager and developer, the first, second and fourth to tester (TASK-42) · 🗄 DB migration `20261005a_run_management.sql` · 🗄 DB migration `20261005b_run_management_permissions.sql`
- **Test results: defects, quality insights and custom reports** — a local defect links several failed attempts and can be pushed to Jira on request (only to a Jira instance an admin has bound to the team on the Jira integration page; a retried push does not open a second issue; status is pulled back with an authenticated refresh — there is no inbound Jira webhook). Flaky (pass↔fail transitions, last 14 days, at least 5 attempts), slow (p50 / p95 of passing runs, last 30 days) and "not executed in window" lists compare runs on the same script, interface, environment and host/device only. Custom reports use a small query language (`script:goto* env:prod status:failed since:7d`) that the server validates and runs before paging; saved reports are private to their author; CSV / print-PDF / PNG export (TASK-42)
- **Test results: share a run, manual runs, live runs** — a run's header can publish a read-only snapshot link (7 days by default, 90 at most, revocable) that shows only titles, counts, statuses, safe labels and durations — never logs, hosts, addresses, evidence links or AI review. Manual runs walk a test case's steps with an explicit outcome and optional evidence per step, resume where they stopped, and finish as exactly one result. A running script shows live progress (polled every 2 s while visible) and can be stopped, which cancels the script itself rather than just releasing the device (TASK-42)
- **Navigation editor: AI Generate button** — next to Validate (dev view, device under control), it opens the AI Generation modal, which had no button since the open-source snapshot. When the interface's entry edge has no action yet, Start now says to set it (Android app: `close_app` then `launch_app` with the package) instead of a pathfinding error (TASK-40)
- **Atlas can build a mobile app's tree** — the `build-tree-mobile` skill is enabled for Atlas (it existed but no agent listed it), and its first step reads the app package from the entry edge instead of an adb shell the agent does not have (TASK-40)
- **Hosts can call Jev too** — a host holds no provider key, so `decisions.decide` / `choose` on a host now send the call to the server (`POST /server/ai/decide`, service key), which resolves it, calls Jev, counts it and writes the decision log, and returns the same result. Before, a host call failed with *no API key* ([AI_ROUTING](../agent/ai/AI_ROUTING.md#decision-models-sharedsrclibaidecisionspy-task-38--task-39))
- **AI decision models: Jev through OpenRouter** — `shared/src/lib/ai/decisions.py` asks a decision model (TypeSafe's Jev, `typesafe/jev-1.13`) to pick one of a closed list of options from a text state, and returns its choice with calibrated probabilities, the cost and the latency; calls are counted in the AI usage stats like any other. It runs on a profile marked as a decision profile, under the usage `text.decision`. It is the shared client for the TASK-38 benchmark, the TASK-33 Pokémon harness and the TASK-39 dataset review ([AI_ROUTING](../agent/ai/AI_ROUTING.md#decision-models-sharedsrclibaidecisionspy-task-38--task-39))
- **Flappy Bird showcase: VirtualPyTest flies Flappy Bird in real time on a web host** — `game/game_flappy` runs one `execute_javascript` action per step that reads the bird, predicts its fall with the game's physics and schedules the next jump, so the ~25 ms an action costs on a host doesn't make it jump late; pipes and loop latency are recorded as KPIs. Userinterface `flappy-web` (built by `shared/src/lib/game/flappy/ui.py`); local runs in `test_scripts/game/local/`
- **Tetris showcase: VirtualPyTest plays Tetris in real time on a web host** — `game/game_tetris` reads the page state once per piece, picks the placement with the El-Tetris heuristic and sends the keys in one batch through the action executor (no navigation per key), then checks the final court read from the captured pixels against the page; rows, score, speed and timing are recorded as KPIs. Userinterface `tetris-web` (built by `shared/src/lib/game/tetris/ui.py`); local runs in `test_scripts/game/local/`
- **AI Dataset: a second AI look settles what the first could not, so the human is asked only for spot-checks** — `triage_batch.py export --second-pass` sends the rows the first AI answer left to a human (disagreement with the run, unsure, confusable pair) and the untriaged ones to a second blind AI answer. A confident answer settles the row; anything still unsettled is *left out of training* instead of going to *Needs you*. The 5% spot-check of the AI's labels stays with the human. The review page shows such rows as "Left out of training (AI unsure)" (TASK-27) · 🗄 DB migration `features/ai-dataset/db/002_triage_excluded.sql`
- **KPI report: each verification card shows its frame's time and the reference name, and a failed scan says when greyscale would have matched** — the card header now carries the capture time (HH:MM:SS.mmm) next to the frame number and offset, and the reference crop is labelled with the reference's name (and filter) instead of "Reference (cropped)". When an image match fails, the same frame is scored again with both the reference and the frame converted to grey (no extra verifier call); each failed card shows that greyscale score, and the header shows a banner when greyscale would have passed. The `greyscale` image filter now means exactly that — both sides grey — instead of a grey reference against a colour frame that silently fell back to colour when the `_greyscale` file was missing. In plain words: you can tell which reference failed at what moment, and the report tells you when switching the reference to greyscale would fix it
- **System Service Status dashboard: the /status page as history, plus the infra VMs** — `/status` probed everything live from the browser and kept nothing, so Grafana had no view of the database, storage, frontend or proxy VMs. The backend server now checks once a minute what `/status` shows (its own services, Supabase, Redis, DB backup age), and a new standard-library VM agent (`infra/vm_agent/`, `vpt-vm-agent.service` on :9110) reports each infra VM's CPU / RAM / disk / load / uptime and its units or containers. To keep the database small, the live status is overwritten in `service_status_current`, `service_status` only gets a row when a status changes, and `vm_metrics` gets one row per VM every 5 minutes (a few MB a month instead of ~1.3 GB per 90 days). Each component reads OK, STOPPED (not running, switched off), DEGRADED, DOWN (failing: crashed, stuck, or running without output — e.g. a heatmap processor with no frame 3 minutes after starting) or UNKNOWN, so a service someone switched off is no longer mixed up with one that broke; a service mid-start or mid-stop reads DEGRADED (restarting) instead of UNKNOWN, here and on `/status`. The new `system-service-status.json` dashboard shows current status per component with when it started (STALE once nothing was reported for 3 minutes), VM resources, a status timeline, availability % and the list of status changes. The agent is installed per VM with `install.sh`, and the server pulls the agents listed in `INFRA_VM_AGENTS` (set on one server per database); the dashboard must be pushed to Grafana. In plain words: Grafana now shows whether the database, storage and other VMs are up, and how busy they are, with history ([VM agent](../../infra/vm_agent/README.md)) · 🗄 DB migration `20261001e_infra_status.sql` · 🗄 DB migration `20261001f_infra_status_compact.sql` · 🗄 DB migration `20261001g_service_status_stopped.sql`
- **2048 showcase: VirtualPyTest plays 2048 on a web host or an Android phone, with the score as a KPI** — one script, `game/game_2048`, reads the board from the captured pixels, lets the heuristic or an LLM pick each move, and records score, max tile, speed and LLM tokens in the run's metadata. The new **2048 Game** Grafana dashboard (`game-2048.json`) charts them per platform and player
- **Grafana alerting: predefined alerts applied with one command** — `scripts/grafana_alerts.py apply --email ...` creates an email contact point and 10 ready-made rules on the data VirtualPyTest already records: host stopped reporting, disk / memory / CPU temperature high, capture or monitor not running, too many incidents on a device, an incident still ongoing (opt-in), a script failing several times in a row, a script whose pass rate dropped against its last 7 days, a device DOWN in fleet health, and — once the System Service Status tables exist — a platform service or infra VM down, infra VM disk / memory high. One digest mail per group (default: every VirtualPyTest alert in one mail, at most one per hour, a reminder per day; `--group-by`, `--group-interval`, `--repeat-interval`), one line per alert instead of Grafana's raw `A=… C=…` values. Rules live in `infra/monitoring/grafana/alerting/vpt-alert-rules.json` and stay editable in Grafana; email needs SMTP set in `grafana.ini` `[smtp]` ([guide](../user-guide/grafana-alerting.md))
- **Fleet Health dashboard: a Live row next to Last night** — the dashboard only showed the daily 07:00 check, so a host that had recovered by mid-morning still read DOWN until the next day while its tile in the app was green. It now has two rows, each with the date/time of its check: **Live**, refreshed every 10 minutes by the new `vpt-fleet-health-live.timer` (`fleet_health_report.py --live`, live rows kept 24 h), which checks only what the incident page cannot see — host offline, monitoring unreachable, capture stalled or lagging — with no AI and without the alerts and script-results fetches; and **Last night**, the 07:00 check with its AI analysis and report. History charts keep using the nightly checks only. The live timer must be installed once on the server VM (`backend_server/config/services/linux/vpt-fleet-health-live.{service,timer}`) and the dashboard pushed to Grafana · 🗄 DB migration `20261001c_fleet_health_run_kind.sql`
- **Live-TV companion (experimental, off by default)** — tells what is on a device's screen (screen type, category, programme) and adds a card under the live video in Rec (Live + Monitoring on): score, goals and line-ups for soccer / rugby / basketball, time left for films and series, a rolling story list for news, speakers / topic / claims for debates and interviews, with web-sourced facts. Speakers come from the channel's name captions; the digest gets speech, subtitles and captions as one timestamped timeline, so a person is only credited with what was said right after their caption. Opt-in per device (`DEVICE{N}_COMPANION_FACTCHECK=true`, capped per hour): each factual claim in a debate is checked with a web search and shown as supported / contradicted / unverified with its source — never a verdict without a source. Packaged as the `companion` feature (`DISABLED_FEATURES=companion` removes it); per host a systemd drop-in sets `ENABLE_COMPANION=true` on `vpt-companion`, per device `DEVICE{N}_COMPANION=true`. Adds web-grounded AI calls (`text.web`, a profile's **web** capability) and the `content-type` training family ([TASK-05](../tasks/TASK-05-live-tv-ai-companion.md), `features/companion/README.md`) · 🗄 DB migration `20260930a_ai_web_capability.sql` · 🗄 DB migration `20260930b_training_family_content_type.sql`
- **Test Prompt runs a prompt two ways: Explore or Run graph** — *Explore* is the AI agent driving the device step by step and judging your acceptance criteria, as before. *Run graph* turns the prompt into a testcase graph and runs it like any testcase; once you **Save as testcase**, later runs replay that graph with no AI call, and **Regenerate** builds a new one. Both modes need Take Control, show live steps and the step overlay on the stream preview, and end with the standard Report and Logs; exploration reports now carry the device's real model and video. The unused `/server/ai/*` and `/host/ai/*` routes left from the old Live AI modal are gone, along with its orphaned hook (TASK-36) · 🗄 DB migration `20261001b_test_prompt_run_mode.sql`
- **Test Prompt is back in the AI menu** — the page (`/test-prompt`: a free-text prompt plus acceptance criteria, run by the Atlas agent) was only reachable by typing its URL on main; its menu entry had only ever existed on the `debug` branch. The `ai-test` feature now registers the entry itself, so a build with that feature disabled shows neither page nor link
- **Test Prompt: a result shows the Userinterface that ran** — the expanded result card lists the user interface above the target screen, so a row with *Target: home_replay* no longer asks which interface that came from (the sidebar list and the top bar already showed it; only the run details did not). The interface was always sent; the `userinterface_name` now lands in `run_details` too, so historical runs reloaded from the DB show it alongside the target
- **Running a testcase takes control of the device, and gives it back when the run ends** — `/server/testcase/execute` (Test Case Builder, AI panel, Campaign Builder block run, MCP `execute_testcase`), `/server/testcase/<id>/execute` and `/server/testcase/execute-from-prompt` (Live AI modal) used to drive the device without any lock, so a script, a deployment or another user could start on it mid-run. It now locks a free device for the run and releases it when the run completes, fails or is aborted; under your own Take Control it runs inside that control and leaves it in place; a device someone else holds answers *device locked* (423) without starting
- **A script run now releases the device when it ends, even if its own user had taken control** — a run started by the user holding Take Control used to leave the device locked to that user afterwards, so queued and API runs waited until someone released it by hand. The release only touches the lock that run ran under; a Take Control taken again in the meantime, or a launch that failed before the script ran, keeps the device
- **Device Models: Add Model offers only models a host can run, and the missing ones are listed** — the Name is now picked from the models hosts support (`DEVICE{n}_MODEL` values) instead of typed free text, which created rows no device could ever use; the server refuses any other name, and a row created that way before is marked *no host runs it*. Every team gets `fire_tv`, `android_tablet`, `ios_mobile`, `tizen`, `camera` and `host_vnc`, so a userinterface can target them. The unused `controllers` column is gone: a device's controllers always came from the host `.env`, never from this page · 🗄 DB migration `20261001a_device_models_catalog.sql`
- **IR, IRTrans or BLE remote on any TV-type device, chosen in the host `.env`** — `stb`, `apple_tv`, `android_tv`, `fire_tv`, `android_tablet`, `tizen` and `camera` devices all accept a lirc IR transmitter (`DEVICE{n}_IR_PATH`), a networked IRTrans box (`DEVICE{n}_IR_IP`, `_IR_LED`, `_IR_TYPE`) or an emulated BLE remote (`DEVICE{n}_BLE_TYPE`); whichever is set is created, the rest are skipped. IRTrans no longer needs a remote learned in the box: without `DEVICE{n}_IR_REMOTE` it sends the same `ir_conf/<IR_TYPE>.json` codes as lirc. An Android TV / Fire TV with IR configured shows the IR panel next to the ADB one, and only then ([INFRARED](../agent/devices/INFRARED.md)) · `d47966813` `14a48c7d8` `0c54bff88`
- **Test Reports: see the full target and who launched a run** — hovering a truncated Target shows the whole host/device name, and an ⓘ icon next to the script name shows who triggered the run (type, user, IP — the report's `Trigger:` line) · `b8c30cdf6e`
- **Run Tests: the standby edge picker shows edges it can't measure in red** — `standby_measurement` lists every edge into standby, judged by its wake direction; one whose wake has no actions or no KPI measurement is red, can't be selected, and its tooltip says what to add in the edge editor
- **Choose the AI model per use case, in Settings → AI** — every AI call now names what it is for (Atlas, Nightwatch, Sherlock, each agent skill, Ask AI, channel banner, language menu, subtitles, DOM generation, test-plan generator, translation, public docs Ask, MCP screenshot description, browser-use...), and routing rules in the database pick the model for each: a server default that a team can override, optionally per host / device model / userinterface, with a fallback chain and an A/B split. Settings → AI has three tabs: **Routing** (the usage tree, where each model comes from, 24 h calls / errors / average latency), **Profiles** (model + parameters, Test button) and **Endpoints** (Anthropic, OpenRouter, OpenAI, MiniMax, MiniMax VLM, Google, or any OpenAI-compatible server such as LM Studio / Ollama / vLLM, reachable from the server or only from the hosts; API key write-only, Test lists models). API keys move from `.env` to a server-side secrets file; hosts no longer hold any and call models through the server. The `AI_*_PROVIDER/MODEL`, `LOCAL_AI_*`, `ANALYZER_OPENROUTER_MODEL`, `DOM_MODEL/EFFORT`, `VLM_*` and provider `*_API_KEY` env keys are no longer read — a one-time import script copies them over so nothing changes on deploy ([AI_ROUTING](../agent/ai/AI_ROUTING.md), [TASK-30](../tasks/TASK-30-ai-usage-routing.md)) · `982f8596b` `638994619` `ea96073f0` `2db17dea1` · 🗄 DB migration `20260929e_ai_routing.sql`
- **Device info is read from a table instead of re-derived on every request** — the latest scan per device now lands in `device_info_latest` when the run is written, so the Device Info page and the info tooltip no longer depend on a query that rescans every `get_info` run ever recorded. The same change the gateway side got in build 8887; behaviour is unchanged, the views return exactly what they did before · `6139cee95` · 🗄 DB migration `20260928a_device_info_latest.sql`
- **Everything admin-only is now a permission you can grant** — the 60 routes that were hard-wired to the admin role (users, teams, roles, settings, code deployment, service restarts, run command, logs, training review, security, CI/CD edit, phone pairing) now check a permission, so a role, team or user can be given exactly one of them from the Roles, Teams and Users pages. Admins hold every permission, so nothing changes for them. 12 new permissions: `device_control:pair`, `org.users:create`, `org.roles:view/edit`, `system.services:restart`, `system.hosts:run_command`, `settings.logs:view`, `settings.cicd:edit`, `ai.training:view/review`, `security:view`, `monitoring.public_ask:view`. Someone given user, team or role administration can only hand out permissions they hold themselves, and cannot edit, delete or create an admin. Page visibility now follows view permissions: unticking one hides the page from the nav and refuses it · `cc83e6cd9` · 🗄 DB migration `20260929b_role_page_view_permissions.sql`
- **Roles page: edit what Tester and Viewer can do** — Org → Roles lists Admin, Tester and Viewer with their permission counts; Tester and Viewer open the same checklist as Edit User. Viewer can only hold view permissions, so unticking one hides that page from every viewer; Admin always holds everything. Role defaults moved from three hard-coded copies (which had drifted apart) into one table, seeded with the values in force today, so nothing changes until someone edits a role. Edits apply within 30 s, no re-login · `aa9d2769b` · 🗄 DB migration `20260929a_role_permissions.sql`
- **Two new roles, Manager and Developer, and roles under your own names** — the role list is now Admin, Manager, Developer, Tester, Viewer. Each role has one preferred name, set on the Roles page and shown everywhere; an external platform sends that same name (`"role": "SuperAdmin"`) and the user API maps it to the role. The same API now also sets the team (`team`, with `replace_teams` to move someone instead of adding a team). A Manager handles the people and team members of their own teams only, never an admin or another manager, and no role can be given the admin-only permissions (roles, team limits, server settings, deploys, hosts, security). Fresh installs now get the role defaults table at all — it was missing from `setup/db/schema/` · `c863cad05` · 🗄 DB migration `20260929c_role_catalog.sql`
- **Teams set limits instead of adding permissions, and devices can be limited per team, role and user** — access now narrows top-down: team › role › user › workspace. A team's permissions are the most its members can ever hold (no limit by default); a user's extra grants must sit inside it, and the Users page colours every permission by where it comes from (role, extra grant, denied, blocked by the team limit) with a legend.
